# Indianapolis

This article is about the capital city of the U.S. state of Indiana. For other uses, see [Indianapolis (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_\(disambiguation\)).

Not to be confused with [Indianópolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian%C3%B3polis) or [Marion County, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana).

**Indianapolis** ([/ˌɪndiəˈnæpəlɪs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/87/LL-Q1860\_%28eng%29-Flame%2C_not_lame-Idianapolis.wav/LL-Q1860\_%28eng%29-Flame%2C_not_lame-Idianapolis.wav.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/87/LL-Q1860_%28eng%29-Flame%2C_not_lame-Idianapolis.wav/LL-Q1860_%28eng%29-Flame%2C_not_lame-Idianapolis.wav.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup> [*IN-dee-ə-NAP-əl-iss*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key)),[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] colloquially known as **Indy**, is the [capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States) and [most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Indiana) of the [U.S. state](18618239.md) of [Indiana](21883857.md) and the [county seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_seat) of [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana). Indianapolis is situated in the state's central [till plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Till_plain) region along the west fork of the [White River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_\(Indiana\)). The city's official slogan, "[Crossroads of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crossroads_of_America)", reflects its historic importance as a transportation hub and its relative proximity to other major North American markets.[^cite_note-Indy_Chamber-12][^cite_note-NPS-13]

According to the [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census), the [Indianapolis proper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_\(balance\)) had a population of 887,642.[^cite_note-QuickFacts22-14] Indianapolis is the [16th-most populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population) city in the United States and the fourth-most populous state capital. In the [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States), it ranks third among cities, after [Chicago](6886.md) and [Columbus, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus,_Ohio). The [Indianapolis metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_metropolitan_area) is the [33rd-most populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_statistical_area) metropolitan statistical area in the United States, home to 2.1 million residents.[^cite_note-IBRC_MSA-15] With a population of more than 2.6 million, the combined statistical area [ranks 28th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_statistical_area).[^cite_note-IBRC_CSA-16] Indianapolis proper covers 368 square miles (950 square kilometers), making it the [18th-most extensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_area) city by land area in the country.

[Indigenous peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) inhabited the area dating to as early as 10,000 BC.[^cite_note-Jones-17] Under the Treaty of St. Mary's (1818), both the [Lenape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenape) and the [Miami Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Nation) relinquished their tribal lands in central Indiana. The Miami Nation of Indiana (and its branch, the Miami Nation of Oklahoma) held primary claim to the territory, which forms part of Cession 99.[^cite_note-Ency-18] In 1821, Indianapolis was established as a [planned city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_city) for the new seat of Indiana's state government. The city was [platted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plat) by [Alexander Ralston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Ralston) and [Elias Pym Fordham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elias_Pym_Fordham) on a one-square-mile (2.6 km<sup>2</sup>) grid. Completion of the [National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Road) and [Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_Road) roads and later arrival of rail solidified the city's position as a major manufacturing and commercial center.[^cite_note-19] Since the 1970 [city-county consolidation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city-county), known as [Unigov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unigov), local government administration operates under the direction of an elected 25-member [city-county council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_City-County_Council) headed by the [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_Indianapolis).

Indianapolis anchors the [28th largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._metropolitan_areas_by_GDP) metropolitan economy in the United States.[^cite_note-FRED-20] Prominent industries include trade, transportation, and utilities; education and health services; professional and business services; government; leisure and hospitality; and manufacturing.[^cite_note-industries-21] The city has notable [niche markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niche_market) in [amateur sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amateur_sports) and [auto racing](1022.md).[^cite_note-employment_industries-22][^cite_note-Naptown-23] As of 2019, Indianapolis has been home to two [major league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada) sports teams, three [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies, eight university campuses, and numerous cultural institutions, including [the world's largest children's museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Children's_Museum_of_Indianapolis).[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25] The city's international reputation rests primarily on the [Indianapolis 500](159018.md), the world's largest single-day sporting event.[^cite_note-Keefer-26][^cite_note-Davidson-27] Among the city's [historic sites and districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places_listings_in_Marion_County,_Indiana), Indianapolis is home to the largest collection of monuments dedicated to veterans and war casualties in the United States outside of [Washington, D.C.](108956.md)[^cite_note-MemorialsMonuments-28][^cite_note-IWM-29]

## Etymology

See also: [List of modern words formed from Greek polis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_modern_words_formed_from_Greek_polis)

The name "Indianapolis" is derived from pairing the state's name, *Indiana* (meaning "Land of the Indians", or simply "Indian Land"[^cite_note-30]), with the suffix *_-polis_*, the [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_language) word for "city". [Jeremiah Sullivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeremiah_Sullivan), justice of the [Indiana Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Supreme_Court), is credited with coining the name.[^cite_note-31] Other names considered were Concord, Delaware, Suwarrow, [Tecumseh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tecumseh), Wayne, and Whetzel.[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33]

## History

Main article: [History of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Indianapolis)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Indianapolis).

### 19th century



An 1820 illustration of Indianapolis



[Alexander Ralston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Ralston)'s "Plat of the Town of Indianapolis" in 1821

In 1816, the year Indiana gained statehood, [U.S. Congress](31756.md) designated four sections of land in central Indiana as the future seat of state government, contingent on tribal removal.[^cite_note-34] Under the [Treaty of St. Mary's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_St._Mary's) (1818), the Delaware and Miami nations relinquished title to these designated lands, with agreement to vacate by 1821.[^cite_note-Ency-18] This tract of land, which was called the [New Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Purchase_\(1818\)), included the site selected for the new state capital in 1820.[^cite_note-35] The indigenous people of the land prior to [systematic removal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_removals_in_Indiana) are the [Miami Nation of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Nation_of_Indiana) ([Miami Nation of Oklahoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Nation_of_Oklahoma)) and Indianapolis makes up part of Cession 99; the primary treaty between the indigenous population and the United States was the [Treaty of St. Mary's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_St._Mary's_\(1818\)) in 1818.[^cite_note-Madison123-36]

The availability of new federal lands for purchase in central Indiana attracted settlers, many of them descendants of families from northwestern Europe. Although many of these first European and American settlers were [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestants), a large proportion of the early [Irish](775859.md) and [German](152735.md) immigrants were [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholics). Few [African Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_American) lived in central Indiana before 1840.[^cite_note-37]

The first [European Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_American) to permanently settle in the area that became Indianapolis were either the McCormick or Pogue families. The McCormicks are generally considered to be the first permanent settlers; however, some historians believe [George Pogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Pogue) and family may have arrived first, on March 2, 1819, and settled in a log cabin along the creek that was later called [Pogue's Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogue's_Run). Other historians have argued as early as 1822 that [John Wesley McCormick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Wesley_McCormick) and his family and employees became the area's first European American settlers, settling near the [White River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_\(Indiana\)) in February 1820.[^cite_note-38]

On January 11, 1820, the [Indiana General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_General_Assembly) authorized a committee to select a site in central Indiana for the new state capital.[^cite_note-39] The state legislature approved the site, adopting the name Indianapolis on January 6, 1821.[^cite_note-founding-1] In April, [Alexander Ralston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Ralston) and [Elias Pym Fordham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elias_Pym_Fordham) were appointed to survey and design a town plan for the new settlement.[^cite_note-40] Indianapolis became a seat of county government on December 31, 1821, when [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana), was established. A combined county and town government continued until 1832 when Indianapolis was incorporated as a town.[^cite_note-The_Encyclopedia_of_Indianapolis-41]

Indianapolis became an incorporated city effective March 30, 1847. [Samuel Henderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Henderson_\(Indianapolis_mayor\)), the city's first mayor, led the new city government, which included a seven-member city council. In 1853, voters approved a new city charter that provided for an elected mayor and a fourteen-member city council. The city charter continued to be revised as Indianapolis expanded.[^cite_note-The_Encyclopedia_of_Indianapolis-41] Effective January 1, 1825, the seat of state government moved to Indianapolis from [Corydon, Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corydon,_Indiana). In addition to state government offices, a [U.S. district court](70235.md) was established at Indianapolis in 1825.[^cite_note-42]

Growth occurred with the opening of the [National Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Road) through the town in 1827, the first major federally funded highway in the United States.[^cite_note-Hyman34-Baer11-43] A small segment of the ultimately failed [Indiana Central Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Central_Canal) was opened in 1839.[^cite_note-Canal-44] The first railroad to serve Indianapolis, the [Jeffersonville, Madison and Indianapolis Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffersonville,_Madison_and_Indianapolis_Railroad), began operation in 1847, and subsequent railroad connections fostered growth.[^cite_note-Britannica-45] [Indianapolis Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Union_Station) was the first of its kind in the world when it opened in 1853.[^cite_note-Union_Station-46]

#### American Civil War and Reconstruction

Main article: [Indianapolis in the American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_in_the_American_Civil_War)

During the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War), Indianapolis was mostly loyal to the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(American_Civil_War\)) cause. [Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Indiana) [Oliver P. Morton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_P._Morton), a major supporter of [President](24113.md) [Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln), quickly made Indianapolis a rallying place for [Union army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Army) troops. On February 11, 1861, [President-elect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President-elect_of_the_United_States) Lincoln arrived in the city, en route to [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) for his [presidential inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_inauguration_of_Abraham_Lincoln), marking the first visit from a president-elect in the city's history.[^cite_note-47] On April 16, 1861, the first orders were issued to form Indiana's first regiments and establish Indianapolis as a headquarters for the state's volunteer soldiers.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49] Within a week, more than 12,000 recruits signed up to fight for the Union.[^cite_note-50]

Indianapolis became a major logistics hub during the war, establishing the city as a crucial military base.[^cite_note-51][^cite_note-Bod443-52] Between 1860 and 1870, the city's population more than doubled.[^cite_note-Britannica-45] An estimated 4,000 men from Indianapolis served in 39 regiments, and an estimated 700 died during the war.[^cite_note-53] On May 20, 1863, Union soldiers attempted to disrupt a statewide Democratic convention at Indianapolis, forcing an adjournment of the proceedings, sarcastically referred to as the [Battle of Pogue's Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Pogue's_Run).[^cite_note-54] Fear turned to panic in July 1863, during [Morgan's Raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan's_Raid) into southern Indiana, but Confederate forces turned east toward [Ohio](22199.md), never reaching Indianapolis.[^cite_note-55] On April 30, 1865, Lincoln's funeral train made a stop at Indianapolis, where an estimated crowd of more than 100,000 people passed the assassinated president's [bier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bier) at the [Indiana Statehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Statehouse).[^cite_note-Bod443-52][^cite_note-Dunn237-56]



A 1909 advertisement for the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway)

Following the Civil War and in the wake of the [Second Industrial Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Industrial_Revolution), Indianapolis experienced tremendous growth and prosperity. In 1880, Indianapolis was the world's third-largest pork packing city, after [Chicago](6886.md) and [Cincinnati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati), and the second-largest railroad center in the U.S. by 1888.[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-Built_Environment-58] In 1889 the city hosted the first [Indianapolis May Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_May_Festival); a classical festival of national reputation that was held annually through 1898.[^cite_note-59] By 1890, the city's population surpassed 100,000.[^cite_note-Britannica-45] Some of the city's most notable businesses were founded during this period of growth and innovation, including [L. S. Ayres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L._S._Ayres) (1872), [Eli Lilly and Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Lilly_and_Company) (1876), [Madam C. J. Walker Manufacturing Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madam_C._J._Walker_Manufacturing_Company) (1910), and [Allison Transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allison_Transmission) (1915).

### 20th century

Some of the city's most prominent architectural features and best known historical events date from the turn of the 20th century. The [Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldiers'_and_Sailors'_Monument_\(Indianapolis\)), dedicated on May 15, 1902, would later become the city's unofficial symbol.[^cite_note-60] [Ray Harroun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Harroun) won the inaugural running of the [Indianapolis 500](159018.md), held May 30, [1911](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1911_Indianapolis_500), at [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway). Indianapolis was one of the hardest hit cities in the [Great Flood of 1913](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Flood_of_1913), resulting in five known deaths[^cite_note-IndyProfile-61][^cite_note-Bell15-62][^cite_note-63] and the displacement of 7,000 families.[^cite_note-BB58182-64]

Once home to 60 automakers, Indianapolis rivaled [Detroit](8687.md) as a center of automobile manufacturing.[^cite_note-automaker-65] The city was an early focus of [labor organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_organization).[^cite_note-Britannica-45] The [Indianapolis streetcar strike of 1913](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_streetcar_strike_of_1913) and subsequent police mutiny and riots led to the creation of the state's earliest labor-protection laws, including a [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage), regular work weeks, and improved working conditions.[^cite_note-d1230-66] The [International Typographical Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Typographical_Union) and [United Mine Workers of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Mine_Workers_of_America) were among several influential labor unions based in the city.[^cite_note-Britannica-45]

As a stop on the [Underground Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underground_Railroad), Indianapolis developed one of the largest African American populations in the [Northern States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(American_Civil_War\)), though this demographic prominence was complicated by the [Great Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Migration_\(African_American\)), as outmigration patterns shifted and other northern cities became destinations.[^cite_note-mumford.albany.edu-67] Led by [D. C. Stephenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D._C._Stephenson), the [Indiana Klan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Klan) became the most powerful political and social organization in Indianapolis from 1921 through 1928, controlling the City Council and the Board of School Commissioners. At its height, more than 40% of native-born white males in Indianapolis claimed membership in the Klan.[^cite_note-68]

While campaigning in the city in 1968, [Robert F. Kennedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_F._Kennedy) delivered one of the most lauded [speeches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_F._Kennedy's_speech_on_the_assassination_of_Martin_Luther_King_Jr.) in 20th century American history following the assassination of [Martin Luther King Jr.](20076.md)[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71] As in most U.S. cities during the [civil rights movement](49001.md), the city experienced strained race relations, including [1969 riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_Indianapolis_riots). A 1971 federal court decision forcing [Indianapolis Public Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Public_Schools) to implement [desegregation busing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desegregation_busing) proved controversial.[^cite_note-72]

During the mayoral administration of [Richard Lugar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Lugar) (1968–1976), the city and county governments consolidated. Known as [Unigov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unigov) (a [portmanteau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portmanteau) of "unified" and "government"), the [city-county consolidation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City-county_consolidation) removed bureaucratic redundancies, captured increasingly [suburbanizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburbanization) tax revenue, and created a [Republican](32070.md) [political machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_machine) that dominated local politics until the early 2000s.[^cite_note-politics-73][^cite_note-74] Effective January 1, 1970, Unigov expanded the city's land area by more than 300 square miles (780 km<sup>2</sup>) and increased its population by some 250,000 people.[^cite_note-75] It was the first major city-county consolidation to occur in the U.S. without a [referendum](51582.md) since the creation of the [City of Greater New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Greater_New_York) in 1898.[^cite_note-76] Lugar is credited with initiating downtown revitalization efforts, overseeing the building of [Market Square Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_Square_Arena), renovations to [Indianapolis City Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_City_Market), and the formation of [Indiana University–Purdue University Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University%E2%80%93Purdue_University_Indianapolis).[^cite_note-77]

Amid the changes in government and growth, the city pursued an aggressive economic development strategy to raise the city's stature as a [sports tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_tourism) destination, known as the Indianapolis Project.[^cite_note-78] During the administration of the city's longest-serving mayor, [William Hudnut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Hudnut) (1976–1992), millions of dollars were invested into sports venues and public relations campaigns. The strategy was successful in landing the [U.S. Olympic Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Olympic_Festival) in 1982, securing the [relocation of the Baltimore Colts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_Colts_relocation_to_Indianapolis) in 1984, and hosting the [1987 Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Pan_American_Games).[^cite_note-Naptown-23]

Beginning in 1992, the mayoral administration of [Stephen Goldsmith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Goldsmith) introduced a number of [austerity](684037.md) measures to address budget shortfalls through [privatization](24661.md) and greater reliance on [public–private partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public%E2%80%93private_partnership). Major downtown revitalization projects continued through the 1990s, including the openings of [Circle Centre Mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circle_Centre_Mall), [Victory Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Field), and [Gainbridge Fieldhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gainbridge_Fieldhouse), as well as ongoing redevelopment of the [Canal and White River State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canal_and_White_River_State_Park) area.[^cite_note-79] [Bart Peterson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bart_Peterson) took office in 2000, the first Democrat elected to the post since [John J. Barton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_J._Barton)'s [1963 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1963_Indianapolis_mayoral_election).[^cite_note-80] The Peterson administration focused on [education reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_reform) and promoting the arts.

Panoramic view of Downtown Indianapolis in 1914

[Downtown Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Indianapolis) in 1914. At left, the [Indiana Statehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Statehouse). At center-left are the Claypool Hotel (foreground) and [Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldiers'_and_Sailors'_Monument_\(Indianapolis\)) (background). The intersection of Illinois St. and the [National Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Street_\(Indianapolis\)) is centered near the bottom of the image. The [Indianapolis News Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_News_Building) and [Hotel Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotel_Washington_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)) are to the left of Washington Street while the [Merchants National Bank Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barnes_and_Thornburg_Building) is visible to the right. At center-right, the [Wholesale District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wholesale_District,_Indianapolis). At far-right, the [Hotel Severin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omni_Severin_Hotel) and [Indianapolis Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Union_Station) clock tower.

### 21st century

In 2001, the mayor's office became the first in the U.S. to authorize [charter schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_schools_in_the_United_States). [Indianapolis Cultural Districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Cultural_Districts) were designated in 2003, followed by the groundbreaking of the [Indianapolis Cultural Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Cultural_Trail) in 2007. Further consolidation of city and county units of government resulted in the establishment of the [Indianapolis Metropolitan Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Metropolitan_Police_Department) in 2007. Later that year, [Greg Ballard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Ballard) succeeded Peterson in a political upset.[^cite_note-81]

The Ballard administration oversaw the lease of the city's parking meters and the sale of the city's water and wastewater utilities with proceeds financing street repairs. Ballard pursued several environmental sustainability efforts, including establishing an office of sustainability, installing 200 miles (320 km) of bike lanes and trails, and spearheading a controversial deal to start an electric [carsharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carsharing) program.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] Two of the city's largest capital projects, the [Indianapolis International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_International_Airport)'s new terminal and [Lucas Oil Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Stadium), were completed in 2008.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85] In 2012, construction began on a \$2 billion tunnel system, a project known as [DigIndy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DigIndy), which was designed to reduce sewage overflows into the city's waterways.[^cite_note-86]

Since 2016, the administration of [Joe Hogsett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Hogsett) has focused on addressing a rise in gun violence and the city's racial disparities. In recent years, significant capital and operational investments have been made in public safety, criminal justice, and public transit. The city also established rental assistance and [food security](216361.md) programs. In 2020, the [George Floyd protests in Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Floyd_protests_in_Indiana) prompted a series of local [police reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_reform_in_the_United_States) and renewed efforts to bolster social services for mental health treatment and homelessness.[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88] In 2021, [a mass shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_FedEx_shooting) occurred at a [FedEx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx) facility on the city's southwest side, killing nine (including the gunman) and injuring seven others.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90]

## Geography



A 2020 [Sentinel-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sentinel-2) true-color image of the [Indianapolis metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_metropolitan_area)

Indianapolis is located in the [East North Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_North_Central_states) region of the [Midwestern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States), about fourteen miles (23 km) south-southeast of Indiana's [geographic center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographic_center).[^cite_note-91] It is situated 98 miles (158 km) northwest of [Cincinnati, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincinnati); 107 miles (172 km) north of [Louisville, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisville,_Kentucky); 164 miles (264 km) southeast of [Chicago, Illinois](6886.md); 168 miles (270 km) west of [Columbus, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus,_Ohio); and 243 miles (391 km) northeast of [St. Louis, Missouri](27687.md).[^cite_note-92] According to the [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Census_Bureau), the [Indianapolis (balance)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_\(balance\)) encompasses a total area of 367.9 square miles (953 km<sup>2</sup>), of which 361.6 square miles (937 km<sup>2</sup>) is land and 6.3 square miles (16 km<sup>2</sup>) is water.[^cite_note-CenPopGazetteer2020-3] It is the [18th-most extensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_area) city by land area in the U.S.

As a [consolidated city-county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city-county), Indianapolis's [city limits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_limits) are coterminous with [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana), except the autonomous and semi-autonomous municipalities outlined in [Unigov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unigov).[^cite_note-Britannica-45][^cite_note-StatsIndiana12-93] Nine [civil townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_township) form the broadest geographic divisions within the city and county; these are [Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Decatur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Lawrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perry_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Pike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pike_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), and [Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana) townships.[^cite_note-Townships-94] The consolidated city-county borders the adjacent counties of [Boone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boone_County,_Indiana) to the northwest; [Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamilton_County,_Indiana) to the north; [Hancock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hancock_County,_Indiana) to the east; [Shelby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_County,_Indiana) to the southeast; [Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_County,_Indiana) to the south; [Morgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan_County,_Indiana) to the southwest; and [Hendricks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendricks_County,_Indiana) to the west.[^cite_note-95]

Between 2.6 million and 11,700 years ago, the Indianapolis area was situated on the southern margin of the [Laurentide ice sheet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurentide_ice_sheet). The erosive advance and retreat of glacial ice produced a flat or gently sloping landscape, known as a [till plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Till_plain). Elevations across Indianapolis vary from about 650 feet (198 m) to 900 feet (274 m) [above mean sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level).[^cite_note-Geology-96] Indianapolis is located in the [West Fork White River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_\(Indiana\)#West_Fork) [drainage basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drainage_basin), part of the larger [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River) watershed via the [Wabash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River) and [Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River) rivers.[^cite_note-97] The White River flows 31 miles (50 km) north-to-south through the city and is fed by some 35 streams, including Eagle Creek, [Fall Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_Creek_\(Indiana\)), Pleasant Run, and [Pogue's Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogue's_Run).[^cite_note-White_River-98] The city's largest waterbodies are artificial [quarry lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quarry_lake) and [reservoirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reservoir).

### Cityscape



[Downtown Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Indianapolis) skyline viewed from the southwest, 2019

Indianapolis is an example of a [planned city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_city). In 1821, the [Indiana General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_General_Assembly) selected a site near the [confluence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confluence) of the [White River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_\(Indiana\)) and [Fall Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_Creek_\(Indiana\)) to serve as the new state capital, adopting a plan co-designed by surveyors [Alexander Ralston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Ralston) and [Elias Pym Fordham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elias_Pym_Fordham).[^cite_note-99] The [grid plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grid_plan) called for a town of 1 square mile (2.6 km<sup>2</sup>) centered on a [traffic circle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traffic_circle) (from which Indianapolis's "Circle City" nickname originates).[^cite_note-100] Four diagonal avenues—[Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Avenue) (northwest), Kentucky (southwest), [Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Avenue,_Indianapolis) (northeast), and Virginia (southeast)—radiated a block from the circle.[^cite_note-101] The city's address numbering system originates at the intersection of [Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Street_\(Indianapolis\)) (running east–west) and [Meridian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian_Street_\(Indianapolis\)) streets (running north–south).[^cite_note-102]

In the 2020 census, Indianapolis was among the 20 largest cities in the U.S. by both population and land area. The city's population density of 2,455 people per square mile (948/km<sup>2</sup>) [ranked 222nd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population) among major U.S. cities. This contrast is evident in Indianapolis's cityscape where [low-density development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low-density_development) patterns dominate and some 14,600 acres (5,900 ha) of farmland remain within its municipal boundaries.[^cite_note-103]

#### Neighborhoods

See also: [List of neighborhoods in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_neighborhoods_in_Indianapolis)



Single-family homes in the [Irvington Terrace Historic District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irvington_Terrace_Historic_District)

For statistical purposes, the consolidated city-county is organized into 99 "neighborhood areas" with most containing numerous individual historic and cultural districts, subdivisions, and some semi-autonomous towns. In total, some 500 self-identified neighborhood associations are listed in the city's Registered Community Organization system.[^cite_note-104] As a result of the city's expansive land area, Indianapolis has a unique [urban-to-rural transect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transect_\(urban\)), ranging from dense urban neighborhoods to suburban [tract housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tract_housing) subdivisions, to rural villages.[^cite_note-105]

Typical of American cities in the Midwest, Indianapolis urbanized in the late 19th and early 20th centuries, resulting in the development of relatively dense, well-defined neighborhoods clustered around streetcar corridors, especially in [Center Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana).[^cite_note-106] Notable [streetcar suburbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streetcar_suburb) include [Broad Ripple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Ripple_Village,_Indianapolis), [Irvington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irvington_Historic_District_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)), and [University Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_Heights,_Indianapolis).[^cite_note-Neighborhoods_and_Communities-107] Starting in the mid-20th century, the [post–World War II economic expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post%E2%80%93World_War_II_economic_expansion) and subsequent [suburbanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburbanization) greatly influenced the city's development patterns. From 1950 to 1970, nearly 100,000 housing units were built in Marion County, most outside Center Township in suburban neighborhoods such as [Castleton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castleton,_Indianapolis), [Eagledale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagledale,_Indianapolis), and [Nora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nora,_Indianapolis).[^cite_note-Neighborhoods_and_Communities-107]

Since the 2000s, [downtown Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Indianapolis) and surrounding neighborhoods have seen increased reinvestment mirroring nationwide market trends, driven by [empty nesters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empty_nesters) and [millennials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennials).[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109] Renewed interest in urban living has been met with some dispute regarding [gentrification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentrification) and [affordable housing](3531662.md).[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112] According to a Center for Community Progress report, neighborhoods like [Cottage Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cottage_Home_Historic_District) and [Fall Creek Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_Creek_Place,_Indianapolis) have experienced measurable gentrification since 2000.[^cite_note-113] The [North Meridian Street Historic District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Meridian_Street_Historic_District) is among the most affluent urban neighborhoods in the U.S., with a mean [household income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Household_income) of \$102,599 in 2017.[^cite_note-114]

#### Historic districts and landmarks

See also: [List of tallest buildings in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Indianapolis)



Segment of the former Indiana Central Canal, an American Water Landmark

The 8-mile-long (13 km) [Indiana Central Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Central_Canal) is the oldest extant artificial facility in the city, dating to the 1830s. Between 1985 and 2001, nearly 1.5 miles (2.4 km) of the former canal in downtown Indianapolis were redeveloped into a cultural and recreational amenity. North of 18th Street, the canal retains much of its original appearance, flowing through the north side neighborhoods of [Riverside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverside,_Indianapolis), [Butler–Tarkington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler%E2%80%93Tarkington,_Indianapolis), [Rocky Ripple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Ripple,_Indiana), and [Broad Ripple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Ripple_Village,_Indianapolis). This segment has been recognized as an [American Water Landmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Water_Landmark) since 1971.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116]

More than 260 properties and historic districts are listed on the [National Register of Historic Places in Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places_listings_in_Marion_County,_Indiana); most sites are located in Center Township, [listed separately](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places_listings_in_Center_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana). This includes nine [National Historic Landmarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark): the [Athenæum (Das Deutsche Haus)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athen%C3%A6um_\(Das_Deutsche_Haus\)), the [Benjamin Harrison Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison_Home), the [Broad Ripple Park Carousel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Ripple_Park_Carousel), [Hinkle Fieldhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinkle_Fieldhouse), the [Indiana World War Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_World_War_Memorial), the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway), the [James Whitcomb Riley House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Whitcomb_Riley_Museum_Home), the [Madam C. J. Walker Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madam_Walker_Legacy_Center), and [Oldfields–Lilly House & Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oldfields).

The [Indianapolis Historic Preservation Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Historic_Preservation_Commission) oversees 13 local historic districts and five conservation areas.[^cite_note-117] Notable districts include [Chatham–Arch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chatham%E2%80%93Arch,_Indianapolis) and [Massachusetts Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_Ave_Cultural_Arts_District), [Cottage Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cottage_Home_Historic_District), [Cumberland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_Historic_District), [Fletcher Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fletcher_Place), [Fountain Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fountain_Square,_Indianapolis), [Herron–Morton Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herron%E2%80%93Morton_Place_Historic_District), [Irvington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irvington_Historic_District_\(Indianapolis\)), [Lockefield Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockefield_Gardens), [Lockerbie Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockerbie_Square_Historic_District), [Monument Circle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monument_Circle), [New Augusta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Augusta_Historic_District), the [Old Northside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Northside_Historic_District), [Ransom Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ransom_Place_Historic_District), [St. Joseph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Joseph_Neighborhood_Historic_District), the [Wholesale District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wholesale_District,_Indianapolis), and [Woodruff Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodruff_Place,_Indianapolis).[^cite_note-118]



The [Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldiers'_and_Sailors'_Monument_\(Indianapolis\)) (completed 1902) stands in contrast to [Salesforce Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salesforce_Tower_\(Indianapolis\)) (completed 1990), reflecting different periods in the development of Indianapolis's skyline.

Following the dedication of the 284-foot (87 m) [Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldiers'_and_Sailors'_Monument_\(Indianapolis\)) in 1902, Indianapolis enacted its first [height restriction laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Height_restriction_laws) to protect views of the monument. In 1962, the 372-foot (113 m) [City-County Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City-County_Building_\(Indianapolis\)) became the first high-rise in the city to surpass the monument's height. The tallest residential buildings in Indianapolis, the twin 295-foot (90 m) 30-floor [Riley Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riley_Towers), were completed in 1963.[^cite_note-119] The 48-floor [Salesforce Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salesforce_Tower_\(Indianapolis\)), completed in 1990, is the city's tallest, with a roof height of 701 feet (214 m). Its distinctive twin antenna masts bring the building's height to 811 feet (247 m).[^cite_note-120]

[Indiana limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_limestone) has been a signature building material in Indianapolis since the 1800s, featured prominently in the city's monuments, churches, commercial, and civic buildings.[^cite_note-121] Examples include [Roberts Park Methodist Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberts_Park_Methodist_Episcopal_Church) (1876), the [Indiana Statehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Statehouse) (1888), the [Majestic Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majestic_Building_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)) (1896), the Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument (1902), [Birch Bayh Federal Building and United States Courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birch_Bayh_Federal_Building_and_United_States_Courthouse) (1905), [Saints Peter and Paul Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saints_Peter_and_Paul_Cathedral_\(Indianapolis\)) (1907), the [Indianapolis Masonic Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Masonic_Temple) (1909), [Old Indianapolis City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Indianapolis_City_Hall) (1910), [Central Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Library_\(Indianapolis\)) (1917), the [Scottish Rite Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Rite_Cathedral_\(Indianapolis\)) (1927), [Circle Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circle_Tower) (1929), the Indiana World War Memorial (1933), the [Indiana State Library and Historical Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_State_Library_and_Historical_Bureau) (1934), [Clowes Memorial Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clowes_Memorial_Hall) (1963), and [OneAmerica Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OneAmerica_Tower) (1982).

#### Parks

See also: [List of parks in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_parks_in_Indianapolis)



[Eagle Creek Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle_Creek_Park) is the largest and most visited park in Indianapolis.

The city of Indianapolis maintains 212 public parks, totaling 11,258 acres (4,556 ha) or about 5.1% of the city's land area.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] [Garfield Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garfield_Park_\(Indianapolis\)), the city's first municipal park, opened in 1876 as Southern Park.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] [George Kessler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Kessler)'s [Indianapolis Park and Boulevard Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Park_and_Boulevard_System) (1909) linked notable parks, such as [Brookside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookside_Park_\(Indianapolis\)), [Ellenberger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irvington_Historic_District_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)#Parks), Garfield, and [Riverside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverside_Park_\(Indianapolis\)), with a system of parkways following the city's waterways.[^cite_note-126] The system's 3,474 acres (1,406 ha) were added to the [National Register of Historic Places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places) in 2003.[^cite_note-127] [Eagle Creek Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle_Creek_Park), Indianapolis's largest and most visited park, ranks among the [largest municipal parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_urban_parks_by_size) in the U.S., covering 4,766 acres (1,929 ha).[^cite_note-128]

Marion County is also home to parks managed by the State of Indiana, including [Fort Harrison State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Harrison_State_Park) and [White River State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_State_Park). Established in 1996, Fort Harrison State Park covers 1,744 acres (706 ha) that are overseen by the [Indiana Department of Natural Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Department_of_Natural_Resources).[^cite_note-129] Since 1979, White River has been owned and operated by the White River State Park Development Commission, a quasi-governmental agency.[^cite_note-130] White River's 250 acres (100 ha) are home to several attractions, including the [Indianapolis Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Zoo) and [White River Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_Gardens). Two [land trusts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_trust) are active in the city managing several sites for [nature conservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_conservation) throughout the region.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132]

### Flora and fauna



A [white-tailed deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-tailed_deer) among [deciduous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deciduous) trees in Indianapolis

Indianapolis is situated in the [Southern Great Lakes forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Great_Lakes_forests) [ecoregion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecoregion) which in turn is located within the larger [temperate broadleaf and mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temperate_broadleaf_and_mixed_forests) [biome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biome), as defined by the [World Wide Fund for Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature).[^cite_note-ecoregion-133] Based on the [U.S. Environmental Protection Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Environmental_Protection_Agency)'s [alternative classification system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ecoregions_in_Indiana), the city is located in the [Eastern Corn Belt Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corn_Belt), an area of the country known for its fertile soil.[^cite_note-134]

Much of the deciduous forests that once covered 98% of the region were [cleared](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation) for agriculture and [urban development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_sprawl), contributing to considerable [habitat loss](1707053.md).[^cite_note-ecoregion-133][^cite_note-Holland,_et_al.-135] Indianapolis's current urban tree canopy averages approximately 33%.[^cite_note-136] A rare example of [old-growth forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old-growth_forest) in the city can be found on 15 acres (6.1 ha) of [Crown Hill Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Hill_Cemetery)'s North Woods in the [Butler–Tarkington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler%E2%80%93Tarkington,_Indianapolis) neighborhood.[^cite_note-137] The cemetery's 555 acres (225 ha) represents the largest green space in [Center Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), home to an abundance of wildlife and some 130 species of trees.[^cite_note-138] Native trees most common to the area include varieties of [ash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fraxinus), [maple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple), and [oak](39696.md).[^cite_note-ecoregion-133] Several [invasive species](67554.md) are also common in Indianapolis, including [tree of heaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ailanthus_altissima), [wintercreeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euonymus_fortunei), [Amur honeysuckle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lonicera_maackii), and [Callery pear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrus_calleryana).[^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140]

A 2016 [bioblitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bioblitz) along three of the city's [riparian corridors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riparian_corridor) found 590 [taxa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxon).[^cite_note-Holland,_et_al.-135] [Urban wildlife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_wildlife) common to the Indianapolis area include mammals such as the [white-tailed deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-tailed_deer), [eastern chipmunk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_chipmunk), [eastern cottontail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_cottontail), and the [eastern grey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_gray_squirrel) and [American red squirrels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_red_squirrel).[^cite_note-ecoregion-133] In recent years, local [raccoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raccoon) and [groundhog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groundhog) populations have increased alongside sightings of [American badgers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_badger), [beavers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_beaver), [mink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_mink), [coyotes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coyote), and [red fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_fox).[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142] Birds native to the area include the [northern cardinal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_cardinal), [wood thrush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wood_thrush), [eastern screech owl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_screech_owl), [mourning dove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mourning_dove), [pileated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pileated_woodpecker) and [red-bellied woodpeckers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-bellied_woodpecker), and [wild turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wild_turkey).[^cite_note-ecoregion-133] Located in the [Mississippi Flyway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_Flyway), the city sees more than 400 migratory bird species throughout the year.[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145] Some 57 species of fish can be found in the city's waterways, including [bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bass_\(fish\)) and [sunfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centrarchidae).[^cite_note-White_River-98] Some federally-designated [endangered](20110668.md) and [threatened species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Threatened_species) are native to the Indianapolis area, including several species of freshwater [mussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mussel), the [rusty patched bumble bee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombus_affinis), [Indiana bat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_bat), [northern long-eared bat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myotis_septentrionalis), and the [running buffalo clover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trifolium_stoloniferum).[^cite_note-146]

In recent years, the [National Wildlife Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Wildlife_Federation) has ranked Indianapolis among the ten most wildlife-friendly cities in the U.S.[^cite_note-147]

### Climate





[Fall foliage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autumn_leaf_color) and a late-winter snowfall on the [Butler University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler_University) campus

Indianapolis has a [hot-summer humid continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot-summer_humid_continental_climate) ([Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *Dfa*), but can be considered a borderline [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate) (Köppen: *Cfa*) using the 27 °F (−3 °C) isotherm. It experiences four distinct seasons.[^cite_note-148] The city lies at the transition between [USDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Agriculture) plant [hardiness zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardiness_zone) 6a and 6b.[^cite_note-149]

Typically, summers are fairly hot, humid, and wet. Winters are generally cold with moderate snowfall. The July daily average temperature is 75.4 °F (24.1 °C). High temperatures reach or exceed 90 °F (32 °C) an average of 18 days each year,[^cite_note-NOAA_Indianapolis_Weatherbox-150] and occasionally exceed 95 °F (35 °C). Spring and autumn are usually pleasant, if at times unpredictable. Midday temperature drops exceeding 30 °F or 17 °C are common during March and April, and instances of very warm days (80 °F or 27 °C) followed within 36 hours by snowfall are not unusual during these months. Winters are cold, with an average January temperature of 28.1 °F (−2.2 °C). Temperatures dip to 0 °F (−18 °C) or below an average of 3.7 nights per year.[^cite_note-NOAA_Indianapolis_Weatherbox-150]

The rainiest months occur in the spring and summer, with slightly higher averages during May, June, and July. May is typically the wettest, with an average of 5.05 inches (12.8 cm) of rain.[^cite_note-NOAA_Indianapolis_Weatherbox-150] Most rain is derived from thunderstorm activity. There is no distinct dry season, although occasional droughts occur. Severe weather is not uncommon, particularly in the spring and summer months. Indianapolis experiences an average of 20 thunderstorm days annually.[^cite_note-151]

Indianapolis's average annual rainfall is 42.4 inches (108 cm). Snowfall averages 25.9 inches (66 cm) per season. Official temperature extremes range from 106 °F (41 °C), set on [July 14, 1936](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1936_North_American_heat_wave),[^cite_note-IndyClimate-152] to −27 °F (−33 °C), set on [January 19, 1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_North_American_cold_wave).[^cite_note-IndyClimate-152][^cite_note-TWC-153]

The [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau) considers Indianapolis as two entities: the consolidated city and [the city's remainder, or balance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_\(balance\)). The consolidated city is coterminous with [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana), except the independent municipalities of [Beech Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beech_Grove,_Indiana), [Lawrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence,_Indiana), [Southport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southport,_Indiana), and [Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speedway,_Indiana).[^cite_note-2010_pop_hh-161] The city's balance excludes the populations of ten semi-autonomous municipalities that are included in totals for the consolidated city.[^cite_note-StatsIndiana12-93] These are [Clermont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clermont,_Indiana), [Crows Nest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crows_Nest,_Indiana), [Homecroft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homecroft,_Indiana), [Meridian Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian_Hills,_Indiana), [North Crows Nest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Crows_Nest,_Indiana), [Rocky Ripple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Ripple,_Indiana), [Spring Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Hill,_Indiana), [Warren Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_Park,_Indiana), [Williams Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Creek,_Indiana), and [Wynnedale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wynnedale,_Indiana).[^cite_note-2010_pop_hh-161][^cite_note-Unigov-2] An eleventh town, [Cumberland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland,_Indiana), is partially included.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163]

In 2015, [Brookings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution) characterized the Indianapolis metropolitan area as a minor-emerging immigrant gateway with a foreign-born population of 126,767, or 6.4% of the total population, a 131% increase from 2000.[^cite_note-164] Much of this growth can be attributed to thousands of Burmese-Chin refugees who have settled in Indianapolis, particularly [Perry Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perry_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), since the late 1990s.[^cite_note-165] Indianapolis is home to one of the largest concentrations of [Chin people](1828130.md) outside of [Myanmar](19457.md) (formerly Burma), with an estimated population ranging from 17,000 to 24,000.[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168]

The [Williams Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Institute) reported that the Indianapolis metropolitan area had an estimated 4.6% [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) adult population in 2020, totaling about 68,000.[^cite_note-169]

At the [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census), Indianapolis had a population of 887,642 and a population density of 2,455 people per square mile (948/km<sup>2</sup>). The estimated population was 880,621 in 2022.[^cite_note-QuickFacts22-14] By population, Indianapolis is the state's largest city and the country's 16th largest.[^cite_note-176]

The [Indianapolis metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_metropolitan_area), officially the Indianapolis–Carmel–Greenwood, IN metropolitan statistical area (MSA), consists of Marion County and the surrounding counties of [Boone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boone_County,_Indiana), [Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_County,_Indiana), [Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamilton_County,_Indiana), [Hancock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hancock_County,_Indiana), [Hendricks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendricks_County,_Indiana), [Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnson_County,_Indiana), [Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison_County,_Indiana), [Morgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan_County,_Indiana), [Shelby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_County,_Indiana), and [Tipton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tipton_County,_Indiana).[^cite_note-OMB_Bulletin_No._23-01-177] In 2020, the metropolitan area's population was 2,111,040, the most populous in Indiana and home to 31% of the state's residents. In 2022, the estimated population was 2,141,779.[^cite_note-IBRC_MSA-15] In 2020, the larger Indianapolis–Carmel–Muncie, IN combined statistical area (CSA) had a population of 2,492,514, home to nearly 37% of Indiana residents across 20 of Indiana's 92 counties.[^cite_note-OMB_Bulletin_No._23-01-177] In 2022, the estimated population was 2,524,790.[^cite_note-IBRC_CSA-16]

In the 2010 census, 97.2% of the Indianapolis population was reported as one race: 61.8% [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_\(U.S._Census\)), 27.5% [Black or African American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_American), 2.1% [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_American) (0.4% Burmese, 0.4% Indian, 0.3% Chinese, 0.3% Filipino, 0.1% Korean, 0.1% Vietnamese, 0.1% Japanese, 0.1% Thai, 0.1% other Asian); 0.3% [American Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States), and 5.5% as other. The remaining 2.8% of the population was reported as [multiracial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_American) (two or more races).[^cite_note-Factfinder2-178] Indianapolis's [Hispanic or Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans) community was 9.4% of the city's population in the 2010 U.S. Census: 6.9% Mexican, 0.4% Puerto Rican, 0.1% Cuban, and 2% as other.[^cite_note-Factfinder2-178] Sometime between 2020 and 2023, Marion County became majority non-white.[^cite_note-nw-179]

In 2010, the median age for Indianapolis was 33.7 years. Age distribution for the city's inhabitants was 25% under the age of 18; 4.4% were between 18 and 21; 16.3% were age 21 to 65; and 13.1% were age 65 or older.[^cite_note-Factfinder2-178] For every 100 females, there were 93 males. For every 100 females age 18 and over, there were 90 males.[^cite_note-180]

The 2010 census reported 332,199 households in Indianapolis, with an average household size of 2.42 and an average family size of 3.08.[^cite_note-Factfinder2-178] Of the total households, 59.3% were family households, with 28.2% of these including the family's own children under the age of 18; 36.5% were husband-wife families; 17.2% had a female householder (with no husband present) and 5.6% had a male householder (with no wife present). The remaining 40.7% were non-family households.[^cite_note-Factfinder2-178] As of 2010, 32% of the non-family households included individuals living alone, 8.3% of these households included individuals age 65 years of age or older.[^cite_note-Factfinder2-178]

The U.S. Census Bureau's 2007–2011 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey) indicated the median household income for Indianapolis city was \$42,704, and the median family income was \$53,161.[^cite_note-AmSurvey-181] Median income for males working full-time, year-round, was \$42,101, compared to \$34,788 for females. Per capita income for the city was \$24,430. 14.7% of families and 18.9% of the city's total population lived below the poverty line. 28.3% of those in poverty were under the age of 18 and 9.2% were age 65 or older.[^cite_note-AmSurvey-181]

### Homelessness

In 2023, a [Point-In-Time Count](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point-In-Time_Count) conducted by the Coalition for Homelessness Intervention and Prevention identified 1,619 homeless individuals in Indianapolis. About 78% of the city's homeless population was sheltered, with 64% living in emergency shelters and 14% in transitional housing. The remaining 22% were unsheltered.[^cite_note-182]

### Religion

| Religion |  | Percent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism) |  | 31% |
| [Evangelicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) |  | 23% |
| [Catholicism](606848.md) |  | 15% |
| Other [Christian](5211.md) |  | 4% |
| [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) |  | 2% |
| [No religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion) |  | 22% |



Interior of [St. John the Evangelist Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._John_the_Evangelist_Catholic_Church_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)), a parish of the [Archdiocese of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Indianapolis) and the oldest Catholic congregation in the city.

Religion has played a central role in shaping the city's healthcare, political, and social service systems.[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-Lantzer-185][^cite_note-186] [Christianity](5211.md) is the prevalent religion in the Indianapolis area. In 2016, the [Public Religion Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Religion_Research_Institute) identified the largest groupings of [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_the_United_States) by faith tradition: 23% [Evangelical Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism), 20% [Mainline Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainline_Protestant), and 11% [Black Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_church), respectively. At 15%, the [Catholic Church](606848.md) was the largest single denomination. Other predominant denominations include [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist) (15%), [Methodists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodist) (8%), [Disciples of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disciples_of_Christ) (5%), [Pentecostals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostal) (5%), [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah's_Witnesses) (3%), and [Lutherans](23371382.md) (2%). About 3% of the population adheres to non-Christian religions. Some 22% of residents identified as religiously "unaffiliated", consistent with the national average of 22.7%.[^cite_note-AVA-183]

Indianapolis is home to roughly 1,200 congregations representing numerous faiths, including some 100 Christian denominations.[^cite_note-187] Some of the city's oldest congregations include [Meridian Street United Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian_Street_United_Methodist_Church) (1821),[^cite_note-188] [Central Christian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Christian_Church_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)) (1833),[^cite_note-Seay-189] [Bethel A.M.E. Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bethel_A.M.E._Church_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)) (1836),[^cite_note-190] [Christ Church Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christ_Church_Cathedral_\(Indianapolis\)) (1837),[^cite_note-191] [St. John the Evangelist Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._John_the_Evangelist_Catholic_Church_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)) (1837),[^cite_note-192] [Second Presbyterian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Presbyterian_Church_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)) (1838),[^cite_note-193] and the [Indianapolis Hebrew Congregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Hebrew_Congregation) (1856).[^cite_note-194] Newer establishments reflect the city's growing religious diversity, including [Masjid Al Mu'mineen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masjid_Al_Mu'mineen) (2001), the [Hindu Temple of Central Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_Temple_of_Central_Indiana) (2006),[^cite_note-195] and the [Indianapolis Indiana Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Indiana_Temple) (2015), located in [Carmel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmel,_Indiana).[^cite_note-196] The [First Church of Cannabis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Church_of_Cannabis) was established in response to Indiana's [Religious Freedom Restoration Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_Freedom_Restoration_Act_\(Indiana\)) in 2015.[^cite_note-Lantzer-185]

Religious denominations headquartered in the Indianapolis area include the [Christian Church (Disciples of Christ)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Church_\(Disciples_of_Christ\)),[^cite_note-Seay-189] the [Free Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Methodist_Church)[^cite_note-197] the [Lutheran Ministerium and Synod – USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran_Ministerium_and_Synod_%E2%80%93_USA), and the [Wesleyan Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesleyan_Church).[^cite_note-Seay-189] Indianapolis is the seat of two [dioceses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocese_of_Indianapolis_\(disambiguation\)).

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Indianapolis)



Indianapolis-based [Lilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Lilly_and_Company) is the city's largest private employer.



[Indiana Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Convention_Center) and [Lucas Oil Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Stadium) in 2024

Indianapolis anchors the [28th largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._metropolitan_areas_by_GDP) metropolitan economy in the United States, with a [gross domestic product](12594.md) of US\$199.2 billion in 2023.[^cite_note-FRED-20] The city's major exports include [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals), motor vehicle parts, medical equipment and supplies, engine and power equipment, and aircraft products and parts.[^cite_note-Indy_Chamber-12] According to the [U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Bureau_of_Labor_Statistics), the largest industries by employment in the [Indianapolis metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_metropolitan_area) are trade, transportation, and utilities; education and health services; professional and business services; government; leisure and hospitality; and manufacturing, respectively. The area's unemployment rate was 3.7% in February 2024.[^cite_note-industries-21]

Three [*Fortune* 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies are based in the city: insurance company [Elevance Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elevance_Health);[^cite_note-198] pharmaceutical company [Lilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Lilly_and_Company);[^cite_note-199] and agrochemical company [Corteva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corteva).[^cite_note-Corteva-200][^cite_note-201] Other notable companies based in the city include [Allison Transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allison_Transmission), [Barnes & Thornburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barnes_&_Thornburg), [Calumet, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calumet,_Inc.), [CountryMark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CountryMark), [Elanco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elanco), [Finish Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finish_Line,_Inc.), [Klipsch Audio Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klipsch_Audio_Technologies), [Lids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lids_\(store\)), [Lucas Oil Products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Products), [OneAmerica Financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OneAmerica_Financial), [Simon Property Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Property_Group), and [Wheaton World Wide Moving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheaton_World_Wide_Moving).

Indianapolis's central location and extensive highway and rail infrastructure have positioned the city as an important logistics center. According to the Indy Chamber, the region was home to some 4,300 establishments employing nearly 110,000 in 2020.[^cite_note-202] [Amazon](90451.md) has a major presence in the Indianapolis metropolitan area, employing 9,000.[^cite_note-203] [FedEx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx) employs 7,000[^cite_note-Logistics-204] workers across 35 facilities in the city, including [FedEx Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Express)'s National Hub, which employs 5,800 workers in sorting, distribution, and shipping at [Indianapolis International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_International_Airport).[^cite_note-205]

Indianapolis anchors one of the largest [life sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_life_sciences) clusters in the United States, notably in the sub-sectors of drugs and pharmaceuticals and agricultural feedstock and chemicals.[^cite_note-BIO2020-206][^cite_note-207] Life sciences employ between 21,200 and 28,700[^cite_note-TEConomy_BioCrossroads-208] among nearly 350 companies located in the region.[^cite_note-209] Pharmaceutical company [Lilly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Lilly_and_Company) is the city's largest private employer, with a workforce of 11,000 in research and development, manufacturing, and executive administration.[^cite_note-Manufacturers-210] Other major employers include [Corteva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corteva),[^cite_note-Corteva-200] [Fortrea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortrea),[^cite_note-211] and [Roche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roche)'s North American headquarters.[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214]

The city's [hospitality industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospitality_industry) has grown in importance in recent years due to an expanding convention business.[^cite_note-conventions-215][^cite_note-Zeigler-216] According to the city's [destination marketing organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destination_marketing_organization), Indianapolis receives 29.2 million visitors annually, generating US\$5.6 billion, and supporting 82,900 jobs.[^cite_note-217] The city's major hospitality facilities are clustered in [downtown Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Indianapolis), including the [Indiana Convention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Convention_Center), [Lucas Oil Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Stadium), and some 8,500 hotel rooms. Major annual conventions include [FDIC International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FDIC_International), the [National FFA Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_FFA_Organization) Convention, [Gen Con](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gen_Con), and the [Performance Racing Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performance_Racing_Industry) Trade Show.[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219][^cite_note-Zeigler-216]

Indianapolis ranks among the fastest high-tech job growth areas in the U.S.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] The metropolitan area is home to 28,500 information technology-related jobs at such companies as [Angi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angi), [Formstack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formstack), [Genesys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesys_\(company\)), [Infosys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infosys),[^cite_note-222] [Ingram Micro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingram_Micro), and [Salesforce Marketing Cloud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salesforce_Marketing_Cloud).[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224] Salesforce has the largest workforce of local tech firms, employing about 2,100 in Indianapolis.[^cite_note-225]

### Manufacturing

Historically, manufacturing has been a critical component of Indianapolis's economy; however, [deindustrialization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deindustrialization) since the mid-20th century has significantly impacted the city's workforce. Indianapolis is typically considered part of the [Rust Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rust_Belt).[^cite_note-226] Between 1990 and 2012, approximately 26,900 manufacturing jobs were lost in the city as it continued diversification efforts and transitioned to a [service economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_economy).[^cite_note-FastTrack-227] [RCA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCA_Corporation) and [Western Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Electric) formerly employed thousands at their Indianapolis manufacturing plants.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]

Once home to 60 automakers, Indianapolis rivaled [Detroit](8687.md) as a center of [automobile manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobile_manufacturing) and design in the early 20th century.[^cite_note-automaker-65] Indianapolis was home to several [luxury car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxury_car) companies, including [Duesenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duesenberg), [Marmon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marmon_Motor_Car_Company), and [Stutz Motor Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stutz_Motor_Company); however, the automakers did not survive the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression_in_the_United_States) of the 1930s.[^cite_note-automobile_industry-230] Detroit's [Big Three automakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(automobile_manufacturers\)) maintained a presence in the city and continued to operate in various capacities until the 2000s: [Ford Motor Company](30433662.md) (1914–1942, 1956–2008),[^cite_note-231] [Chrysler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrysler) (1925–2005), and [General Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors) (1930–2011).[^cite_note-automobile_industry-230]

Indianapolis is home to [Allison Transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allison_Transmission)'s headquarters and manufacturing facilities, employing 2,500 in the design and production of automatic transmissions and hybrid propulsion systems.[^cite_note-Manufacturers-210] [Rolls-Royce North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolls-Royce_North_America) dates its local presence to the establishment of the [Allison Engine Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allison_Engine_Company) in 1915. Its Indianapolis Operations Center has a workforce of 4,000 in aircraft engine development and manufacturing.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-Manufacturers-210] Other major manufacturing employers include [Allegion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegion) and [RTX Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation).[^cite_note-Manufacturers-210] In 2016, [Carrier Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_Corporation) [announced the closure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier_Air_Conditioner_move_to_Mexico) of its Indianapolis plant, moving 1,400 manufacturing jobs to Mexico.[^cite_note-233] Carrier later negotiated with the incoming Trump administration to save some jobs. The company's local workforce numbers 800 in gas furnace production.[^cite_note-234]

## Arts and culture

### Visual arts



[Robert Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Indiana)'s *[LOVE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_\(Indianapolis\))* at the Indianapolis Museum of Art in 2008

With a permanent collection of 54,000 works, the [Indianapolis Museum of Art at Newfields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Museum_of_Art_at_Newfields) is the city's primary art museum. It is among the [largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_art_museums) and oldest art museums in the United States, tracing its roots back to the [Art Association of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Association_of_Indianapolis), which was founded in 1883.[^cite_note-235] The museum's 152-acre (62 ha) Newfields' campus includes [Oldfields–Lilly House & Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oldfields) and the [Virginia B. Fairbanks Art & Nature Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_B._Fairbanks_Art_&_Nature_Park).
