Established in 1902, the [Herron School of Art and Design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herron_School_of_Art_and_Design)'s first core faculty included [Impressionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impressionist) painters belonging to the [Hoosier Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosier_Group). [Eskenazi Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidney_and_Lois_Eskenazi_Hall) at [IU Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IU_Indianapolis) has been home to the school since 2005, hosting five public galleries and numerous programs throughout the year.[^cite_note-236]

Located in [Broad Ripple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Ripple_Village,_Indianapolis), the [Indy Art Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Art_Center) houses the Marilyn K. Glick School of Art, galleries, and a [sculpture garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sculpture_garden). The center hosts and manages art classes, exhibitions, art fairs, and outreach programs throughout the year.[^cite_note-237] The [Harrison Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison_Center) in the [Old Northside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Northside_Historic_District) and the [Murphy Art Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murphy_Art_Center) in [Fountain Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fountain_Square,_Indianapolis) house several galleries and artist studios and participate in [First Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Friday_\(public_event\)) events.

Located in [downtown Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Indianapolis), the [Eiteljorg Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eiteljorg_Museum) is home to a diverse collection of [visual arts by indigenous peoples of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visual_arts_by_indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) and [Western American Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_American_Art). The museum hosts numerous lectures, artist residencies, special exhibitions, and events annually.[^cite_note-238]

In 2021, there were more than 3,000 recorded works of [public art in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_public_art_in_Indianapolis). More than one-third of those are concentrated in the downtown Indianapolis area, including dozens belonging to the [Indiana University–Purdue University Indianapolis Public Art Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University%E2%80%93Purdue_University_Indianapolis_Public_Art_Collection), the [Indiana Statehouse Public Art Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Statehouse_Public_Art_Collection), or the [Indianapolis Cultural Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Cultural_Trail). [Murals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural) are the most popular medium of public art found in the city.[^cite_note-239]

Since 2020, [Ganggang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganggang) has been a prominent supporter of [Black artists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African-American_art) in the city. Their annual art fair "BUTTER" is a multi-day art exhibition that takes place over [Labor Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Day) weekend.[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243]

### Performing arts



The [Indianapolis Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Symphony_Orchestra) performing at [Hilbert Circle Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilbert_Circle_Theatre) in 2019



[Madam Walker Legacy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madam_Walker_Legacy_Center) opened on [Indiana Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Avenue) in 1927 as a cultural center for the city's African American community.

[^cite_note-244]

Several of the city's most prominent performing arts venues and organizations are located in the downtown area, including the [Hilbert Circle Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilbert_Circle_Theatre)[^cite_note-245] (home to the [Indianapolis Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Symphony_Orchestra))[^cite_note-246] and the [Indiana Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Theatre_\(Indianapolis,_Indiana\)) (home to the [Indiana Repertory Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Repertory_Theatre)).[^cite_note-247] Other notable venues near the central business district include [The Cabaret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Cabaret),[^cite_note-248] [Indianapolis Artsgarden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Artsgarden),[^cite_note-249] [Phoenix Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenix_Theatre_\(Indianapolis\)),[^cite_note-250] [Slippery Noodle Inn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slippery_Noodle_Inn),[^cite_note-251] and Everwise Amphitheater at [White River State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_State_Park), the city's largest outdoor venue.[^cite_note-252]

The [Mass Ave Cultural Arts District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_Ave_Cultural_Arts_District) is home to [Old National Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_National_Centre), the oldest stagehouse in Indianapolis, having opened in 1910.[^cite_note-253] The performing arts center features the 2,500-seat Murat Theatre, the 2,000-seat Egyptian Room, and the 600-seat Corinthian Hall. Mass Ave is also home to the [Athenæum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athen%C3%A6um_\(Das_Deutsche_Haus\)), Basile, District, and IF theaters, all of which host the annual [Indianapolis Theatre Fringe Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Theatre_Fringe_Festival), or "IndyFringe".[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] Hedback Community Theatre in [Herron–Morton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herron%E2%80%93Morton_Place_Historic_District) is home to Footlite Musicals[^cite_note-256] and [Epilogue Players](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epilogue_Players) theater companies.[^cite_note-257]

The [Madam Walker Legacy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madam_Walker_Legacy_Center) opened in the heart of the city's [African-American neighborhood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African-American_neighborhood) on [Indiana Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Avenue) in 1927. The building's theater hosted [vaudeville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaudeville) shows and anchored the Indiana Avenue jazz scene from the 1920s through the 1960s.[^cite_note-258] "The Avenue" produced greats such as [David Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Baker_\(composer\)), [Slide Hampton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slide_Hampton), [Freddie Hubbard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freddie_Hubbard), [J. J. Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._J._Johnson), [James Spaulding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Spaulding), and the Montgomery Brothers ([Buddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddy_Montgomery), [Monk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monk_Montgomery), and [Wes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wes_Montgomery)).[^cite_note-Montgomery-259] Wes Montgomery is considered one of the most influential [jazz guitarists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz_guitarist) of all time,[^cite_note-Montgomery-259][^cite_note-260] and is credited with popularizing the "Naptown Sound".[^cite_note-261]

Local performing arts organizations include the [Indianapolis Baroque Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Baroque_Orchestra),[^cite_note-262] [Indianapolis Chamber Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Chamber_Orchestra),[^cite_note-263] [Indianapolis Men's Chorus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Men's_Chorus),[^cite_note-264] [Indianapolis Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Opera),[^cite_note-265] and [Indianapolis Youth Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Youth_Orchestra). Other notable venues include Butler University's [Clowes Memorial Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clowes_Memorial_Hall)[^cite_note-266] and [Melody Inn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melody_Inn_\(nightclub\)) in [Butler–Tarkington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler%E2%80%93Tarkington,_Indianapolis), and the Frank and Katrina Basile Opera Center and the [Jazz Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz_Kitchen) in [Meridian–Kessler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian-Kessler,_Indianapolis).[^cite_note-267] The city's [Broad Ripple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad_Ripple_Village,_Indianapolis) and [Fountain Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fountain_Square,_Indianapolis) neighborhoods are known for local live music, home to dozens of venues.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269]

Indianapolis is home to a variety of national professional musical organizations, including the [American Piano Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Piano_Awards),[^cite_note-270] [Bands of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bands_of_America), [Drum Corps International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_Corps_International), and the [Percussive Arts Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Percussive_Arts_Society).[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] Annual music festivals and competitions held in the city include the [Drum Corps International World Class Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drum_Corps_International_World_Class_Champions), [Indianapolis Early Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Early_Music),[^cite_note-273] and [Indy Jazz Fest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Jazz_Fest).[^cite_note-274] The quadrennial [International Violin Competition of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Violin_Competition_of_Indianapolis) is considered among the most prestigious of its kind in the world.[^cite_note-275]

### Literary arts



A mural memorializing [Kurt Vonnegut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurt_Vonnegut) on [Mass Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_Ave_Cultural_Arts_District), completed by local artist Pamela Bliss in 2011

From about 1870 to 1920, Indianapolis was at the center of the [Golden Age of Indiana Literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Age_of_Indiana_Literature). Several notable poets and writers based in the city achieved national prominence and critical acclaim during this period, including [James Whitcomb Riley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Whitcomb_Riley), [Booth Tarkington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booth_Tarkington), and [Meredith Nicholson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meredith_Nicholson).[^cite_note-NPS-13][^cite_note-276] Perhaps the city's most acclaimed twentieth-century writer was [Kurt Vonnegut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurt_Vonnegut), known for his darkly satirical and controversial bestselling novel *[Slaughterhouse-Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slaughterhouse-Five)*.[^cite_note-277] Vonnegut became known for including at least one character in his novels from Indianapolis.[^cite_note-Vonnegut-278][^cite_note-Memorial_Library-279]

A key figure of the [Black Arts Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Arts_Movement), Indianapolis resident [Mari Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mari_Evans) was among the most influential of the twentieth century's black poets.[^cite_note-280] Indianapolis is home to bestselling [young adult fiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_adult_fiction) writer [John Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Green), known for his critically acclaimed 2012 novel *[The Fault in Our Stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Fault_in_Our_Stars)*, set in the city.[^cite_note-281] Notable sites in Indianapolis include the [James Whitcomb Riley Museum Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Whitcomb_Riley_Museum_Home), the [Ray Bradbury Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Bradbury_Center) at [IU Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IU_Indianapolis), and the [Kurt Vonnegut Museum and Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurt_Vonnegut_Museum_and_Library).[^cite_note-Memorial_Library-279]

### Attractions

See also: [List of attractions and events in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_attractions_and_events_in_Indianapolis)



"[Bucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Specimens_of_Tyrannosaurus#%22Bucky%22:_TCM_2001.90.1)", a juvenile *[Tyrannosaurus](30467.md)* specimen at the Children's Museum of Indianapolis in 2010

[The Children's Museum of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Children's_Museum_of_Indianapolis) is the world's largest children's museum with 433,000 square feet (40,227.02 m<sup>2</sup>) of exhibit space and a collection of over 120,000 artifacts.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] Due to its leadership and innovations, the museum is a world leader in its field.[^cite_note-284] In 2023, it was one of the region's most popular attractions, drawing about 1.2 million visitors.[^cite_note-Attractions-285]

The [Indianapolis Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Zoo) houses more than 1,400 animals of 235 species while the adjoining [White River Gardens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_River_Gardens) contains more than 50,000 plants of nearly 3,000 species, respectively.[^cite_note-2020_Annual_Report-286] The zoo is a leader in animal conservation and research, recognized for its biennial [Indianapolis Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Prize) award.[^cite_note-287] It is among the largest privately funded zoos in the U.S.[^cite_note-288] and one of the city's most visited attractions, with 1.1 million guests in 2023.[^cite_note-Attractions-285]

Located inside the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway), the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway_Museum) exhibits an extensive collection of [auto racing](1022.md) memorabilia showcasing various motorsports and automotive history.[^cite_note-289] Daily grounds and track tours originate from the museum. Located at the [National Collegiate Athletic Association](60706.md) headquarters, the [NCAA Hall of Champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Hall_of_Champions) contains exhibits on [collegiate athletics in the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_athletics_in_the_United_States)[^cite_note-Opsahl_Verderame-290]

The [Benjamin Harrison Presidential Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison_Presidential_Site) includes the archives and restored home of the 23rd president of the U.S., [Benjamin Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Harrison).[^cite_note-291] Harrison is buried about 3 miles (4.8 km) north of the site at [Crown Hill Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Hill_Cemetery). Other notable interments at the cemetery include three [U.S. vice presidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_vice_presidents_of_the_United_States) ([Fairbanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_W._Fairbanks), [Hendricks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_A._Hendricks), and [Marshall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_R._Marshall)), notorious American gangster [John Dillinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Dillinger),[^cite_note-292] and [First Lady](10847.md) [Caroline Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caroline_Harrison).[^cite_note-293] State-specific historical institutions based in Indianapolis include the [Indiana Historical Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Historical_Society), [Indiana Humanities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Humanities), the [Indiana Jewish Historical Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Jewish_Historical_Society), [Indiana Landmarks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Landmarks),[^cite_note-294] the [Indiana Medical History Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Medical_History_Museum), and the [Indiana State Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_State_Museum).

The [Indiana World War Memorial Historic District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_World_War_Memorial_Historic_District) in downtown Indianapolis is home to more than a dozen memorials and monuments to United States veterans, including the national headquarters of the [American Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Legion), the [Indiana War Memorial Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_War_Memorial_Museum), and the [Soldiers' and Sailors' Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldiers'_and_Sailors'_Monument_\(Indianapolis\)).[^cite_note-295] Combined, it is the largest war memorial project in the United States,[^cite_note-macleod-296] encompassing 24 acres (9.7 ha).[^cite_note-Mitchell-297] Other notable sites in the city include [Crown Hill National Cemetery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Hill_National_Cemetery), the [Indiana 9/11 Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_9/11_Memorial), the [Medal of Honor Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medal_of_Honor_Memorial_\(Indianapolis\)), and the [USS *Indianapolis* National Memorial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Indianapolis_\(CA-35\)#Memorials).



[Indiana Farmers Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Farmers_Coliseum) during the 2015 [Indiana State Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_State_Fair)

Indianapolis hosts numerous annual fairs, festivals, and parades.[^cite_note-festivals-298] The largest is the [Indiana State Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_State_Fair), regularly drawing more than 800,000 attendees each August. Held during the "[Month of May](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Month_of_May_\(Indianapolis_500\))", the [500 Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/500_Festival) features over 30 programs and events leading up to the [Indianapolis 500](159018.md).[^cite_note-Attractions-285] Other notable events include the [Indy Pride Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Pride_Festival) and the [Marion County Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County_Fair_\(Indiana\)) in June,[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] the [Indiana Black Expo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Black_Expo) Summer Celebration in July,[^cite_note-301] the [Historic Irvington Halloween Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Irvington_Halloween_Festival) in October,[^cite_note-302] and the [Circle of Lights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circle_of_Lights) during the [holiday season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiday_season).[^cite_note-303]

### Cuisine



[Indianapolis City Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_City_Market), founded in 1821

Indianapolis has an emerging food scene as well as established eateries.[^cite_note-304] Founded in 1821 as the city's [public market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marketplaces#Types), the [Indianapolis City Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_City_Market) has served the community from its current building since 1886. Before [World War II](32927.md), the City Market was home to meat and vegetable vendors. As consumer habits evolved and residents moved from the central city, City Market transitioned from a traditional marketplace to a [food hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_hall).[^cite_note-305] The AMP and The Garage food halls opened in 2021.[^cite_note-306]

Situated in the [Corn Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corn_Belt), Indianapolis has maintained close ties to farming and food production. [Urban agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_agriculture) in the city dates to the 1930s, when [Flanner House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flanner_House) began teaching Black arrivals how to farm on vacant lots during the [Great Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Migration_\(African_American\)). Within a few years, more than 200 families were tending 600 garden plots on nearly 100 acres (40 ha) of land on the city's near north side.[^cite_note-307] Urban agriculture has made a comeback in recent years in an effort to alleviate [food deserts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_deserts).[^cite_note-308] According to the city's Office of Sustainability, there were 129 community farms and gardens in 2020.[^cite_note-Thrive-309] As of 2020, several [farmers' markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmers'_market) have been established throughout Indianapolis.[^cite_note-310]

Distinctive local dishes include [pork tenderloin sandwiches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pork_tenderloin_sandwich),[^cite_note-311] [sugar cream pie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_cream_pie), and [beef Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beef_Manhattan), invented in Indianapolis.[^cite_note-312] [Wonder Bread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wonder_Bread) and [Choc-Ola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choc-Ola) also originate in the city. Longstanding local eateries include [Long's Bakery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long's_Bakery), [Mug-n-Bun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mug-n-Bun), Shapiro's Deli,[^cite_note-313] [The Workingman's Friend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Workingman's_Friend), and [St. Elmo Steak House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Elmo_Steak_House).[^cite_note-314] St. Elmo was recognized with the [America's Classics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_James_Beard_America's_Classics) award by the [James Beard Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Beard_Foundation) in 2012.[^cite_note-315] In 2008, the Indianapolis metropolitan area had the highest concentration of [chain restaurants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chain_restaurants) per capita in the U.S.[^cite_note-316] Locally based chains include [MCL Restaurant & Bakery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MCL_Restaurant_&_Bakery), [Noble Roman's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noble_Roman's), and [Steak 'n Shake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steak_'n_Shake). The city's growing immigrant population has contributed to a rise in [global cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_cuisine), with some 800 ethnic restaurants opening in recent years.[^cite_note-317]

In 2016, *[Condé Nast Traveler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cond%C3%A9_Nast_Traveler)* named Indianapolis the "most underrated food city in the U.S.", while ranking Milktooth as one of the best restaurants in the world.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319] *[Food & Wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_&_Wine)* called Indianapolis the "rising star of the Midwest".[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321] Several Indianapolis chefs and restaurateurs have been semifinalists in the [James Beard Foundation Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Beard_Foundation_Award) in recent years.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323] [Microbreweries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microbreweries) have become a staple in the city, increasing fivefold since 2009.[^cite_note-324] There are now about 50 craft brewers in Indianapolis, with [Sun King Brewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_King_Brewing) being the largest.[^cite_note-325]

### Film and television

Main category: [Films set in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Films_set_in_Indianapolis)

Main category: [Television shows set in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Television_shows_set_in_Indianapolis)



[Hilbert Circle Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilbert_Circle_Theatre), the first purpose-built [movie palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Movie_palace) in Indianapolis

[^cite_note-326]

Indianapolis natives have left a mark on the entertainment industry, most notably during the [Classical Hollywood cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Hollywood_cinema) era. [James Baskett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Baskett) received an [Academy Honorary Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Honorary_Award) in [1948](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Academy_Awards) for his role in [Walt Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney)'s *[Song of the South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_of_the_South)*, becoming the first Black male Oscar recipient. [Sid Grauman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sid_Grauman), one of the founders of the [Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Motion_Picture_Arts_and_Sciences), received an Academy Honorary Award in [1949](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_Academy_Awards), recognized for raising the standard for film exhibition. Perhaps the most famous actor from the Indianapolis area is Academy Award-nominee, [Steve McQueen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_McQueen), who was born in [Beech Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beech_Grove,_Indiana). Other Academy Award nominees from the city include costume designer [Gloria Gresham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Gresham), actress [Marjorie Main](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Main), and actor [Clifton Webb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton_Webb).[^cite_note-327]

The city's sports venues have served as backdrops for such films as *[Hoosiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosiers_\(film\))* (1986) and *[Eight Men Out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eight_Men_Out)* (1988).[^cite_note-328] The city's largest contribution to [popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_culture), the [Indianapolis 500](159018.md), has influenced entertainment for decades, [referenced in film, television, video games, and other media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_500_in_film_and_media).[^cite_note-329] Three motion pictures filmed at the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway) include *[Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speedway_\(1929_film\))* (1929), *[To Please a Lady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/To_Please_a_Lady)* (1950), and *[Winning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winning_\(film\))* (1969).[^cite_note-330] Other motion pictures at least partially filmed in the city include *[Going All the Way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Going_All_the_Way)* (1997), *[Palindromes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palindromes_\(film\))* (2004), *[Saving Star Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saving_Star_Wars)* (2004), *[Amanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanda_\(2009_film\))* (2009), *[Walter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_\(2015_film\))* (2015), *[The MisEducation of Bindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_MisEducation_of_Bindu)* (2019),[^cite_note-331] *[Athlete A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athlete_A)* (2020), and *[Our Father](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Father_\(2022_film\))* (2022). *Hoosiers* and *[Ringling Brothers Parade Film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ringling_Brothers_Parade_Film)* (1902) were added to the [National Film Registry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Film_Registry) in 2001 and 2021, respectively.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]

Television programs that have been shot on location in the city include *[100 Days to Indy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/100_Days_to_Indy)*, *[American Ninja Warrior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Ninja_Warrior)*,[^cite_note-334] *[Antiques Roadshow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiques_Roadshow_\(American_TV_program\))*,[^cite_note-335] *[College GameDay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_GameDay_\(football_TV_program\))*,[^cite_note-336] *[Cops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cops_\(TV_series\))*,[^cite_note-337] *[Diners, Drive-Ins and Dives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diners,_Drive-Ins_and_Dives)*,[^cite_note-Film-338] *[Extreme Makeover: Home Edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_Makeover:_Home_Edition),*[^cite_note-339] *[Gaycation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaycation_\(TV_series\))*,[^cite_note-340] *[Ghost Hunters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_Hunters_\(TV_series\))*,[^cite_note-341] *[Good Bones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Bones_\(TV_series\))*,[^cite_note-342] *[Good Morning America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Morning_America)*,[^cite_note-343] *[Hard Knocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Knocks_\(documentary_series\))*,[^cite_note-344] *[House Hunters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Hunters)*,[^cite_note-345] *[Late Night with Jimmy Fallon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Night_with_Jimmy_Fallon)*,[^cite_note-346] *[Man v. Food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_v._Food)*,[^cite_note-Film-338] *[Parks and Recreation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parks_and_Recreation)*,[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] *[Say I Do](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Say_I_Do_\(TV_series\))*,[^cite_note-349] *[SportsCenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SportsCenter)*,[^cite_note-350] *[Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Today_\(American_TV_program\))*,[^cite_note-351] and *[What Would You Do?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/What_Would_You_Do%3F_\(2008_TV_program\))*[^cite_note-352]

Annual [film festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_festival) held in Indianapolis include the Circle City Film Festival, [Heartland International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heartland_International_Film_Festival),[^cite_note-353] [Indy Film Fest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Film_Fest),[^cite_note-festivals-298] the [Indianapolis Jewish Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Jewish_Film_Festival), and the Indianapolis LGBT Film Festival. Founded in 2018, the Indy Shorts International Film Festival is one of 34 film festivals in the world used to qualify for the Academy Awards.[^cite_note-354]

Film Indy was established in 2016 to support local visual artists, filmmakers, and aspiring filmmakers; recruit film and television-related marketing opportunities to the region, and provide resources for producers interested in filming in the city.[^cite_note-Film-338] Since 2016, more than 350 film and media projects have been produced in the Indianapolis region with a collective economic impact of \$24.1 million and the creation of 1,900 local jobs.[^cite_note-355]

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Indianapolis)

### Professional



[Lucas Oil Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Stadium), home of the [Indianapolis Colts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Colts)



[Gainbridge Fieldhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gainbridge_Fieldhouse), home to the [Indiana Pacers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Pacers) and [Indiana Fever](240214.md)

The [Indianapolis Colts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Colts) of the [National Football League](21211.md) have been based in the city since [relocating from Baltimore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_Colts_relocation_to_Indianapolis) in 1984. The Colts' tenure in Indianapolis has produced 11 division championships, two conference titles, and two [Super Bowl](27718.md) appearances. [Pro Football Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Football_Hall_of_Fame) inductees [Tony Dungy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Dungy) and [Peyton Manning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peyton_Manning) led the team to win [Super Bowl XLI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XLI) in 2007.[^cite_note-356] [Lucas Oil Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Stadium) replaced the team's first home, the [RCA Dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCA_Dome), in 2008.[^cite_note-357]

Founded in 1967, the [Indiana Pacers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Pacers) began in the [American Basketball Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Basketball_Association) (ABA), joining the [National Basketball Association](22093.md) (NBA) when the leagues [merged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABA-NBA_merger) in 1976. Before joining the NBA, the Pacers won three ABA division titles and three ABA championships ([1970](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_ABA_Playoffs), [1972](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_ABA_Playoffs), [1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_ABA_Playoffs)), led by [Naismith Basketball Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naismith_Basketball_Hall_of_Fame) inductees [Mel Daniels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mel_Daniels), [Roger Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Brown_\(basketball,_born_1942\)), and [George McGinnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_McGinnis). Since the merger, the Pacers have made 10 appearances in the [NBA Eastern Conference finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Eastern_Conference_finals). In [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999%E2%80%932000_Indiana_Pacers_season), Hall of Fame inductees [Larry Bird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Bird) and [Reggie Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reggie_Miller) led the team to its first [NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_NBA_Finals) appearance.[^cite_note-358] The team returned to the NBA Finals in [2025](76692117.md), led by [Rick Carlisle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Carlisle) and [Tyrese Haliburton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrese_Haliburton).

Since their debut in 2000, the [Indiana Fever](240214.md) of the [Women's National Basketball Association](57721.md) (WNBA) have won three conference titles, made three appearances in the [WNBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNBA_Finals), and won one championship. Hall of fame inductees [Lin Dunn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Dunn) and [Tamika Catchings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamika_Catchings) led the Fever to their first championship title in [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_WNBA_Finals).[^cite_note-359] The Fever and Pacers share [Gainbridge Fieldhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gainbridge_Fieldhouse), which replaced [Market Square Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_Square_Arena) in 1999.[^cite_note-360]

The [Indianapolis Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Indians) are a [Minor League Baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_League_Baseball) team of the [International League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_League) and the [Triple-A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple-A_\(baseball\)) affiliate of the [Pittsburgh Pirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh_Pirates).[^cite_note-361] Established in 1902, the Indians are among the oldest teams in Minor League Baseball.[^cite_note-362] The Indians have won 26 division titles, 14 league titles, and seven championships, most recently in 2000. [Victory Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Field) replaced [Bush Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bush_Stadium) as the team's home in 1996.[^cite_note-363]

[Indy Eleven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Eleven), a professional soccer team in the second-division [USL Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_Championship), play their home matches at [Carroll Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IU_Michael_A._Carroll_Track_&_Soccer_Stadium).[^cite_note-364] The [Fishers Event Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishers_Event_Center) in the suburb of [Fishers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishers,_Indiana) is home to the [Indy Fuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Fuel) (a minor league ice hockey team in the [ECHL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ECHL)),[^cite_note-365] [Indy Ignite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Ignite) (a member of the [Pro Volleyball Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Volleyball_Federation)), and [Fishers Freight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishers_Freight) (a member of the [Indoor Football League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indoor_Football_League)).

### Amateur



[Butler Bulldogs men's basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler_Bulldogs_men's_basketball) at [Hinkle Fieldhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinkle_Fieldhouse)

Indianapolis has been called the "Amateur Sports Capital of the World".[^cite_note-Britannica-45][^cite_note-366] The [National Collegiate Athletic Association](60706.md) (NCAA), the main governing body for U.S. collegiate sports, and the [National Federation of State High School Associations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Federation_of_State_High_School_Associations) are based in the city.[^cite_note-Opsahl_Verderame-290] Indianapolis is home to two [NCAA athletic conferences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NCAA_conferences): the [Horizon League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horizon_League) ([D-I](669931.md)) and the [Great Lakes Valley Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes_Valley_Conference) ([D-II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_II)). The city is also home to three national sport governing bodies, as recognized by the [U.S. Olympic & Paralympic Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Olympic_&_Paralympic_Committee): [USA Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Football); [USA Gymnastics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Gymnastics); and [USA Track & Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Track_&_Field).[^cite_note-367]

Two NCAA D-I athletic programs are based in Indianapolis: the [Butler Bulldogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler_Bulldogs) of the [Big East Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_East_Conference) and the [IU Indy Jaguars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IU_Indy_Jaguars) of the Horizon League.[^cite_note-IUPUI-368] The [University of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indianapolis) is a D-II school; the [Greyhounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Greyhounds) compete in the Great Lakes Valley Conference. [Marian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marian_University_\(Indiana\)) athletics compete in the National Association of Intercollegiate Athletics's [Crossroads League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crossroads_League).

Traditionally, Butler's [Hinkle Fieldhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinkle_Fieldhouse) was the hub for [Hoosier hysteria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoosier_hysteria), a general excitement for the game of basketball throughout the state, specifically the [Indiana High School Boys Basketball Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_High_School_Boys_Basketball_Tournament).[^cite_note-369] Hinkle, a [National Historic Landmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark), opened in 1928 as the world's largest basketball arena, with seating for 15,000.[^cite_note-370] It is regarded as "Indiana's Basketball Cathedral".[^cite_note-371] The [Indiana High School Athletic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_High_School_Athletic_Association) is based in Indianapolis.[^cite_note-372]

### Motorsports



An [open-wheel car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-wheel_car) crossing the Yard of Bricks during practice for the [2012 Indianapolis 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Indianapolis_500)

Indianapolis is a global center for [auto racing](1022.md), home to numerous motorsports facilities and events, two sanctioning bodies ([INDYCAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INDYCAR) and [United States Auto Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Auto_Club)), and more than 500 motorsports-related companies.[^cite_note-373] Indianapolis, or Indy, is a [metonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metonymy) for auto racing, particularly when referring to [American open-wheel car racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_open-wheel_car_racing).[^cite_note-374]

Completed in 1909 as an automotive test track, the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway) is a [National Historic Landmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark) and the world's largest [sports venue by capacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sports_venues_by_capacity), with 235,000 permanent seats.[^cite_note-375] Since 1911, the 2.5-mile-long (4.0 km) rectangular oval has hosted the [Indianapolis 500](159018.md), an open-wheel automobile race. It is contested as part of the [IndyCar Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IndyCar_Series) and traditionally held over [Memorial Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_Day) weekend. Steeped in [tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_500_traditions), the race is considered the world's largest single-day sporting event and part of the [Triple Crown of Motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_Crown_of_Motorsport).[^cite_note-Keefer-26][^cite_note-Davidson-27] The track also hosts the [Grand Prix of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prix_of_Indianapolis) in May and NASCAR's [Brickyard 400](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brickyard_400) and [Pennzoil 250](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennzoil_250) in July.[^cite_note-376]

[Lucas Oil Indianapolis Raceway Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Oil_Indianapolis_Raceway_Park) (in nearby [Brownsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brownsburg,_Indiana)) contains a 2.5-mile-long (4.0 km) road course, a 4,400-foot-long (1,300 m) [dragstrip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragstrip), and a 0.69-mile-long (1.11 km) [oval short track](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oval_track_racing#Short_track). Each [Labor Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Day) weekend, the facility hosts the [NHRA U.S. Nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHRA_U.S._Nationals), the largest and most prestigious [drag racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drag_racing) event in the world.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] The city's [Indianapolis Speedrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Speedrome) is believed to be the oldest operating [figure 8 racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Figure_8_racing) venue in the U.S.[^cite_note-379]

### Events

Beyond its many auto races, Indianapolis hosts numerous other sporting events throughout the year, including the [NFL Scouting Combine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NFL_Scouting_Combine) in February, the [500 Festival Mini-Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/500_Festival_Mini-Marathon) in May,[^cite_note-380] the [Circle City Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circle_City_Classic) in September,[^cite_note-381] the [Monumental Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Monumental_Marathon) in November, and the [Big Ten Football Championship Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Ten_Football_Championship_Game) and [Indy Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Classic) in December. Indianapolis also regularly hosts the [NCAA Division I men's](507399.md) and [women's basketball](536018.md) Final Fours, most recently in [2026](76465965.md) and [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_NCAA_Division_I_women's_basketball_tournament), respectively.[^cite_note-382]

Notable past events hosted in the city include the [U.S. Clay Court Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Clay_Court_Championships) (1969–1987), the [National Sports Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Olympic_Festival) (1982), the [NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_All-Star_Game) ([1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_NBA_All-Star_Game) and [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_NBA_All-Star_Game)), the [Pan American Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan_American_Games) ([1987](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Pan_American_Games)), the [Indianapolis Tennis Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Tennis_Championships) (1988–2009), the [World Artistic Gymnastics Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Artistic_Gymnastics_Championships) ([1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_World_Artistic_Gymnastics_Championships)), [WrestleMania VIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WrestleMania_VIII) (1992), the [World Rowing Championships](1148937.md) ([1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_World_Rowing_Championships)), the [United States Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Grand_Prix) (2000–2007), the [World Police and Fire Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Police_and_Fire_Games) (2001), the [FIBA Basketball World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Basketball_World_Cup) ([2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIBA_World_Championship)), [Super Bowl XLVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XLVI) (2012), the [College Football Playoff National Championship](39217672.md) ([2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_College_Football_Playoff_National_Championship)), and the [WNBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNBA_All-Star_Game) ([2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_WNBA_All-Star_Game)).

## Government

Main article: [Government of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Indianapolis)

See also: [List of mayors of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_Indianapolis)



[City-County Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City-County_Building_\(Indianapolis\))



[Indiana Statehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Statehouse)



[Birch Bayh Federal Building and United States Courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birch_Bayh_Federal_Building_and_United_States_Courthouse)

Indianapolis—officially the Consolidated City of Indianapolis and [Marion County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana)—has a [consolidated city-county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city-county) form of government, a status it has held since 1970 under [Indiana Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Code)'s [Unigov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unigov) provision. Many functions of the municipal and county governments are combined, though some remain separate.[^cite_note-Unigov-2] The city has a [strong mayor–council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government) system of government which oversees six administrative departments. Marion County also contains some 60 taxing units, nine separate [civil township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_township) governments, and seven special-purpose [municipal corporations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipally_owned_corporation).[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384]

The executive branch is headed by an elected mayor who serves as the chief executive of both the city and county.[^cite_note-Mayor-385] [Indianapolis City-County Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_City-County_Council) is the legislative body and consists of 25 members, all of whom represent geographic districts. The mayor and council members are elected to unlimited four-year terms.[^cite_note-Mayor-385][^cite_note-386] Executive and legislative functions are based from the [City-County Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City-County_Building_\(Indianapolis\)). The judiciary consists of a circuit court and superior court with four divisions and 32 judges.[^cite_note-Unigov-2] Each of the county's nine civil townships elects its own [township trustee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_township_trustee), three-member board, assessor, and a constable and small claims court judge, all of whom serve four-year terms.[^cite_note-Townships-94]

Since its move from [Corydon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corydon,_Indiana) in 1825, Indianapolis has served as the [capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States) and seat of Indiana's state government. The [Indiana Statehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Statehouse) houses the executive, legislative, and judicial branches of state government, including the office of the [Governor of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Indiana), the [Indiana General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_General_Assembly), and the [Indiana Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Supreme_Court). Most state departments and agencies are headquartered in the neighboring [Indiana Government Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Government_Center_North) complex.[^cite_note-387] The [Indiana Governor's Residence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Governor's_Residence) is on [Meridian Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian_Street_\(Indianapolis\)) in the [Butler–Tarkington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler-Tarkington,_Indianapolis) neighborhood, about 5 miles (8.0 km) north of downtown. In the [Indiana House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_House_of_Representatives), Indianapolis is split between 16 districts.[^cite_note-388] In the [Indiana Senate](4964463.md), the city is split between nine districts.[^cite_note-389]

The [Birch Bayh Federal Building and U.S. Courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birch_Bayh_Federal_Building_and_United_States_Courthouse) houses the [U.S. District Court for the Southern District of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._District_Court_for_the_Southern_District_of_Indiana). Most federal field offices are located in the [Minton-Capehart Federal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minton-Capehart_Federal_Building). From 1906 to 1991, the [U.S. Army](32087.md) operated [Fort Benjamin Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Benjamin_Harrison) in neighboring [Lawrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence,_Indiana). About 5,000 federal employees work for the [Defense Finance and Accounting Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Finance_and_Accounting_Service), headquartered near the former base.[^cite_note-390] Indianapolis is split between two of Indiana's nine [congressional districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_district): [Indiana's 7th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana's_7th_congressional_district), represented by [André Carson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Carson), and [Indiana's 6th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana's_6th_congressional_district), represented by [Jefferson Shreve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_Shreve).

### Politics

Further information: [Marion County, Indiana § Politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County,_Indiana#Politics)



[2023 Indianapolis mayoral election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Indianapolis_mayoral_election) by precinct:\
**[Hogsett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Hogsett) ([D](5043544.md)):**      50–60%      60–70%      70–80%      80–90%      >90%\
**[Shreve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_Shreve) ([R](32070.md)):**      50–60%      60–70%      70–80%\
**Tie:**      50%\
&#x20;     No votes

Until fairly recently, Indianapolis was considered one of the most [conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_the_United_States) major cities in the U.S.[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-politics-73] According to 2014 research published in the *[American Political Science Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Political_Science_Review)*, the city's policy preferences are less conservative than the national mean when compared with other large U.S. cities.[^cite_note-392] While Indianapolis as a whole leans Democratic, the southern third of the city, consisting of [Decatur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perry_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), and [Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana) townships, trends Republican.[^cite_note-393]

[Republicans](32070.md) held the mayor's office for 32 years (1967–1999), and controlled the City-County Council from its inception in 1970 to 2003.[^cite_note-politics-73] In the [2000 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_United_States_presidential_election), Marion County voters narrowly selected [George W. Bush](3414021.md) over [Al Gore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Gore) by a margin of 1.3%, but voted in favor of [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) by a margin of 1.9% in the [2004 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_United_States_presidential_election). Presidential election results have increasingly favored Democrats, with Marion County voters selecting [Joe Biden](145422.md) over [Donald Trump](4848272.md) in the [2020 United States presidential election](48410011.md), 63.3–34.3%.[^cite_note-394] Incumbent mayor [Democrat](5043544.md) [Joe Hogsett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Hogsett) faced Republican State Senator [Jim Merritt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Merritt_\(American_politician\)) and [Libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian_Party_\(United_States\)) Doug McNaughton in the [2019 Indianapolis mayoral election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indianapolis_mayoral_election). Hogsett was elected to a second term, with 72% of the vote.[^cite_note-395] The [2019 City-County Council elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indianapolis_City-County_Council_election) expanded Democratic control of the council, flipping six seats to hold a 20–5 [supermajority](638313.md) over Republicans.[^cite_note-396]

### Public safety



[Indianapolis Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Fire_Department) Station 13



[Indianapolis Metropolitan Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Metropolitan_Police_Department) vehicle

The [Indianapolis Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Fire_Department) (IFD) comprises seven battalions with 44 fire stations.[^cite_note-397] IFD provides [mutual aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual_aid_\(emergency_services\)) to the excluded municipalities of [Lawrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence,_Indiana) and [Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speedway,_Indiana), as well as [Decatur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), [Pike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pike_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana), and [Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_Township,_Marion_County,_Indiana) townships (all of which operate separate fire departments). IFD directs operations for Indiana Task Force 1 (IN-TF1), one of 28 [FEMA Urban Search and Rescue Task Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FEMA_Urban_Search_and_Rescue_Task_Force) teams in the U.S.[^cite_note-IFD-398]

The [Indianapolis Metropolitan Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Metropolitan_Police_Department) (IMPD) is the city's primary [law enforcement agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement_agency). IMPD's jurisdiction covers Marion County, excluding the municipalities of [Beech Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beech_Grove,_Indiana), Lawrence, [Southport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southport,_Indiana), and Speedway (all of which operate separate forces). In 2020, IMPD had 1,700 sworn police personnel and 250 civilian employees across six districts.[^cite_note-399] In 2022, the Community Justice Campus opened, housing the [Marion County Sheriff's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_County_Sheriff's_Office), a new courthouse, jail, and mental health and substance abuse clinic.[^cite_note-400]

The [Indiana National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_National_Guard)'s major command units and joint headquarters staff are based at the former [Stout Army Air Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stout_Army_Air_Field) on the city's southwest side.[^cite_note-401]

Unlike other major Midwest cities like [Detroit](8687.md) and [Chicago](6886.md), the homicide rate for Indianapolis remained below the national average throughout the 1990s.[^cite_note-402] Homicides hit a spike in 1998 when the city reached 162 murders. Murders drastically decreased in the following years but spiked again in 2006 with 153 murders.[^cite_note-403] Until 2019, annual criminal homicide numbers had grown each year since 2011, reaching record highs from 2015 to 2018.[^cite_note-criminal_homicides-404] With 144 criminal homicides, 2015 surpassed 1998 as the year with the most murder investigations in the city. With 159 criminal homicides, 2018 stands as the most violent year on record in the city.[^cite_note-criminal_homicides-404] [FBI](11127.md) data showed a 7% increase in violent crimes committed in Indianapolis, outpacing the rest of the state and country.[^cite_note-405] Law enforcement has blamed increased violence on a combination of root causes, including poverty, substance abuse, and mental illness.[^cite_note-406]

## Education

See also: [List of schools in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_schools_in_Indianapolis)

### Primary and secondary schools



Established in 1864, [Shortridge High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shortridge_High_School) is Indiana's oldest free public high school.

[^cite_note-407]

Nine [K–12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%E2%80%9312) public [school districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_district) serve residents of the consolidated city as defined by the U.S. Census Bureau (which includes the balance and included cities, and does not include the excluded cities):[^cite_note-408]

- [Franklin Township Community School Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Township_Community_School_Corporation)

- [Indianapolis Public Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Public_Schools)

- [Metropolitan School District of Decatur Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_School_District_of_Decatur_Township)

- [Metropolitan School District of Lawrence Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_School_District_of_Lawrence_Township)

- [Metropolitan School District of Pike Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_School_District_of_Pike_Township)

- [Metropolitan School District of Warren Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_School_District_of_Warren_Township)

- [Metropolitan School District of Washington Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_School_District_of_Washington_Township)

- [Metropolitan School District of Wayne Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_School_District_of_Wayne_Township)

- [Perry Township Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perry_Township_Schools)

[Beech Grove City Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beech_Grove_City_Schools) and [Speedway School Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speedway_School_Town) only include sections of excluded cities.

Indianapolis Public Schools is the largest district in the city, enrolling about 23,000 students across 60 schools.[^cite_note-409] In 2015, the district began contracting with [charter organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_school) and nonprofit managers to operate failing schools.[^cite_note-410] About 63% of the district's students attend traditional neighborhood or [magnet schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnet_school), while the remaining 37% are enrolled in independently managed schools.[^cite_note-Weddle-411][^cite_note-412] About 18,000 students are enrolled in tuition-free charter schools sponsored by the Indianapolis Mayor's Office of Education Innovation and Indianapolis Charter School Board.[^cite_note-413]

Indianapolis is home to two state-supported [residential schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boarding_school), the [Indiana School for the Blind and Visually Impaired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_School_for_the_Blind_and_Visually_Impaired) and [Indiana School for the Deaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_School_for_the_Deaf). According to the [Indiana Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Department_of_Education), about 75 private, parochial, and independent charter schools operate throughout Marion County. Roman Catholic and Christian parochial primary and secondary schools are most prevalent.[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415] Notable independent private schools include the [International School of Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_School_of_Indiana),[^cite_note-416] the [Orchard School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Orchard_School_\(Indianapolis\)),[^cite_note-417] and [Park Tudor School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Tudor_School)[^cite_note-418] in [Meridian Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meridian_Hills,_Indiana).[^cite_note-419]

### Colleges and universities



The [Indiana University Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University_Indianapolis) [Campus Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IUPUI_Campus_Center)

The city is home to two four-year [public university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_university) campuses: [Indiana University Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University_Indianapolis) and [Purdue University in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purdue_University_in_Indianapolis). The two institutions jointly operated [Indiana University–Purdue University Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_University%E2%80%93Purdue_University_Indianapolis) (IUPUI) until its dissolution in 2024.[^cite_note-IUPUI-368] IU Indianapolis is an [R1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_research_universities_in_the_United_States)[^cite_note-420] [urban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_university) [research university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_university) that enrolls about 25,000 students in 330 different bachelor's, master's, and doctoral degree programs. It is home to 17 degree-granting schools, including the main campus of the [IU School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IU_School_of_Medicine), the largest medical school by enrollment in the U.S.[^cite_note-AAMC-421][^cite_note-Kirchhoff-422] Purdue University in Indianapolis is considered an extension of [its flagship campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purdue_University) in West Lafayette.

[Ivy Tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_Tech_Community_College_of_Indiana), Indiana's statewide [community college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_college) system, enrolls some 21,000 full-time students at its Downtown Indianapolis campus.[^cite_note-423] Other public institutions with [satellite campuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_campus) in the city include Ball State University's [Estopinal College of Architecture and Planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estopinal_College_of_Architecture_and_Planning),[^cite_note-424] [Purdue Polytechnic Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purdue_Polytechnic_Institute), and [Vincennes University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincennes_University).[^cite_note-425]

Founded in 1855, [Butler University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler_University) is a [secular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularity), [private university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_university) that serves about 5,000 students from its [Butler–Tarkington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butler%E2%80%93Tarkington,_Indianapolis) campus.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-Largest_Colleges_and_Universities-427] [Indiana Tech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Institute_of_Technology) maintains a branch campus in the city.[^cite_note-428] Two [seminaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seminaries) are located in the city: [Bishop Simon Bruté College Seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_Simon_Brut%C3%A9_College_Seminary) and [Christian Theological Seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Theological_Seminary).[^cite_note-429] Three religiously affiliated universities based in the city are [Indiana Bible College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Bible_College), the [University of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indianapolis),[^cite_note-Largest_Colleges_and_Universities-427] and [Marian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marian_University_\(Indiana\)).[^cite_note-Largest_Colleges_and_Universities-427] The [College of Biblical Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_of_Biblical_Studies) and [Indiana Wesleyan University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Wesleyan_University) operate satellite sites in Indianapolis.[^cite_note-430]

### Libraries

Main article: [Indianapolis Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Public_Library)



The Simon Reading Room at [Central Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Library_\(Indianapolis\))

Founded in 1873, the [Indianapolis Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Public_Library) (IndyPL) consists of the [Central Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Library_\(Indianapolis\)) and 24 branches throughout Marion County.[^cite_note-431] Central Library's special collections include the Center for Black Literature & Culture, the [Chris Gonzalez Collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Gonzalez_Collection), and the [Nina Mason Pulliam Indianapolis Special Collections Room](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nina_Mason_Pulliam_Indianapolis_Special_Collections_Room). The library collection contains nearly 1.7 million materials staffed by 410 full-time employees and has a circulation of 14.6 million, making it the ninth largest library by circulation in the U.S.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433]

Indianapolis is also home to the [Indiana State Library and Historical Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_State_Library_and_Historical_Bureau), the state's largest public library.[^cite_note-434] Notable academic libraries in the city include IU Indianapolis's [University Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IUPUI_University_Library) and Butler University's [Irwin Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irwin_Library).

## Media

Main article: [Media in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Indianapolis)

### Print

Indianapolis's primary daily newspaper is the *[Indianapolis Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Indianapolis_Star)*.[^cite_note-435] Defunct major newspapers include the *[Indianapolis News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_News)*, an evening publication which printed its last edition in 1999;[^cite_note-436] and the *[Indianapolis Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Times)*, which ceased publication in 1965.[^cite_note-437] Additional publications include *[Indianapolis Monthly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Monthly)*, a regional lifestyle publication;[^cite_note-438] *[Indianapolis Business Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Business_Journal)*, a weekly business newspaper;[^cite_note-439] and *[NUVO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NUVO_\(newspaper\))*, an [alternative weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_newspaper) that became digital-only in 2019.[^cite_note-440]

Indianapolis's [ethnic media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_media) include the *[Indianapolis Recorder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Recorder)*, a weekly newspaper that primarily serves the city's African American community;[^cite_note-441] *[Indiana Minority Business Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Minority_Business_Magazine)*, a quarterly publication;[^cite_note-442] and *La Voz de Indiana*, a biweekly newspaper printed in English and [Spanish](26825.md).[^cite_note-443]

### Broadcast



The studios of [WYXB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WYXB) and [WIBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WIBC_\(FM\)) at [Emmis Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmis_Communications) in 2006

The Indianapolis [television market area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_market_area) is served by 11 full-power stations, including [WTTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTTV) 4 ([CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS)),[^cite_note-444] [WRTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WRTV) 6 ([ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company)),[^cite_note-445] [WISH-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WISH-TV) 8 ([The CW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_CW)),[^cite_note-446] [WTHR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTHR) 13 ([NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC)),[^cite_note-447] [WFYI-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WFYI_\(TV\)) 20 ([PBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS)),[^cite_note-448] [WNDY-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNDY-TV) 23 ([MyNetworkTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MyNetworkTV)), [WHMB-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHMB-TV) 40 ([Univision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Univision)), [WCLJ-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WCLJ-TV) 42 ([Bounce TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bounce_TV)), [WXIN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WXIN) 59 ([Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company)),[^cite_note-449] [WIPX-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WIPX-TV) 63 ([Ion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ion_Television)), and [WDTI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WDTI) 69 ([Daystar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daystar_\(TV_network\))). Indianapolis natives [Jane Pauley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jane_Pauley) and [David Letterman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Letterman) launched their [Emmy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmy_Award)-winning broadcasting careers in [local television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_programming), Pauley with WISH-TV and Letterman with WTHR, respectively.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] Sports talk program *[The Pat McAfee Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pat_McAfee_Show)* broadcasts from the city, airing weekdays on [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN).[^cite_note-452]

Dozens of [commercial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_broadcasting) [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AM_broadcasting) and [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FM_broadcasting) [radio stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_broadcasting) serve the Indianapolis area, including [WCBK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WCBK-FM) ([country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music)), [WEDJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WEDJ) ([Regional Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Mexican_music)), [WFBQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WFBQ) ([classic rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_rock)),[^cite_note-453] [WFMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WFMS) (country),[^cite_note-454] [WHHH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHHH) ([urban contemporary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_contemporary_music)), [WIBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WIBC_\(FM\)) ([news](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-news_radio)/[talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk_radio)),[^cite_note-455] [WJJK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WJJK) ([classic hits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_hits)), [WLHK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WLHK) (country), [WNTS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNTS) (classic Regional Mexican), [WNDX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNDX) ([mainstream rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainstream_rock)), [WNTR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNTR) ([adult contemporary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adult_contemporary_music)), [WOLT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WOLT) ([classic alternative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classic_alternative)), [WSYW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSYW) ([Spanish](26825.md) adult contemporary), [WTLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WTLC-FM) ([urban adult contemporary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_adult_contemporary)),[^cite_note-456] [WYXB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WYXB) (adult contemporary), [WZPL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WZPL) ([contemporary hits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_hit_radio)), and [WZRL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WZRL) (mainstream urban). Since 1983, WFBQ has been the [flagship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flagship_\(broadcasting\)) station for the popular nationally [syndicated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_syndication) radio program *[The Bob & Tom Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bob_&_Tom_Show)*.[^cite_note-457]

[Sports radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_radio) stations include [WFNI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WFNI) ([ESPN Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_Radio)),[^cite_note-458] [WNDE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNDE) ([Fox Sports Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Sports_Radio)),[^cite_note-459] and [WXNT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WXNT) ([Infinity Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infinity_Sports_Network)).[^cite_note-460] WFNI (formerly WIBC, currently broadcasting on WIBC-HD3 and its FM translators) is the flagship of the [Indianapolis Motor Speedway Radio Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Motor_Speedway_Radio_Network).[^cite_note-461] [Religious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_broadcasting) stations include [WBRI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WBRI),[^cite_note-462] [WGNR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WGNR-FM), [WWDL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WWDL), and [WYHX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WYHX). [WICR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WICR) is the [campus radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campus_radio) station at the [University of Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Indianapolis).[^cite_note-463] [Classical Music Indy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Music_Indy) produces and syndicates [classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_music) programming for WICR.[^cite_note-464] [Metropolitan Indianapolis Public Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Indianapolis_Public_Media) operates [WFYI-FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WFYI-FM), the region's [NPR](29697232.md) affiliate.[^cite_note-465]

## Infrastructure

### Transportation

Main article: [Transportation in Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Indianapolis)



[Interstates 65](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_65_in_Indiana) and [70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_70_in_Indiana) run concurrently on the eastern perimeter of [downtown Indianapolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Indianapolis).



An [IndyGo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IndyGo) bus at the [Julia M. Carson Transit Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_M._Carson_Transit_Center)



[Indianapolis International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_International_Airport) Colonel H. Weir Cook Terminal Civic Plaza

Indianapolis's transportation infrastructure consists of a complex network that includes a local public bus system, several private intercity bus providers, [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) passenger rail service, four freight rail lines, [four primary and two auxiliary Interstate Highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Interstate_Highways_in_Indiana), two airports, a heliport, bikeshare system, 115 miles (185 km) of bike lanes,[^cite_note-Thrive-309] and 110 miles (177 km) of trails and greenways.[^cite_note-transport-466][^cite_note-Thrive-309] Private [ridesharing companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridesharing_companies) [Lyft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyft) and [Uber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber) as well as [taxicabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxicabs) operate in the city.[^cite_note-467] Launched in 2018, electric [scooter-sharing systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scooter-sharing_system) operating in Indianapolis include [Bird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird_Global), [Lime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lime_\(transportation_company\)), and Veo.[^cite_note-468]

[Urban sprawl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_sprawl) and the absence of a comprehensive regional public transit system have contributed to Indianapolis residents driving more vehicle miles per capita than any other U.S. city.[^cite_note-Gibson-469] According to the 2016 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey), 83.7% of working residents in the city commuted by driving alone, 8.4% carpooled, 1.5% used public transportation, and 1.8% walked. About 1.5% used all other forms of transportation, including taxicab, motorcycle, and bicycle. About 3.1% of working city residents worked at home.[^cite_note-470] In 2015, 10.5 percent of Indianapolis households lacked a car, which decreased to 8.7 percent in 2016, the same as the national average in that year. Indianapolis averaged 1.63 cars per household in 2016, compared to a national average of 1.8.[^cite_note-471]

Four primary Interstate Highways intersect the city: [Interstate 65](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_65_in_Indiana), [Interstate 69](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_69_in_Indiana), [Interstate 70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_70_in_Indiana), and [Interstate 74](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_74_in_Indiana). The metropolitan area also has two auxiliary Interstate Highways: a [beltway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beltway) ([Interstate 465](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_465)) and [connector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connector_\(road\)) ([Interstate 865](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_865)). The [Indiana Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Department_of_Transportation) manages all Interstates, [U.S. Highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Numbered_Highway_System), and [state roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_state_roads_in_Indiana) within the city. The city's Department of Public Works maintains more than 3,400 miles (5,500 km) of local streets in addition to alleys, sidewalks, curbs, and 510 bridges.[^cite_note-Dwyer-472]

Sidewalks are absent from nearly 2,000 miles (3,200 km) of the city's roadways,[^cite_note-Dwyer-472] contributing to Indianapolis's low [walkability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walkability) among peer U.S. cities.[^cite_note-473] However, city officials have increased investments in bicycle and pedestrian infrastructure in recent years.[^cite_note-Gibson-469] About 110 miles (180 km) of trails and greenways form the core of the city's [active transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_mobility) network, connecting into 115 miles (185 km) of on-street bike lanes.[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-Thrive-309] Popular routes include the [Fall Creek Greenway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_Creek_Greenway), [Monon Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monon_Trail), and [Pleasant Run Greenway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleasant_Run_Greenway).[^cite_note-transport-466] The privately managed [Indianapolis Cultural Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_Cultural_Trail) operates [Indiana Pacers Bikeshare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Pacers_Bikeshare), the city's [bicycle-sharing system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicycle-sharing_system), which consists of 525 bicycles and 50 stations.[^cite_note-475]

[IndyGo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IndyGo) operates and manages the city's [public bus system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transport_bus_service), including [bus rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_rapid_transit), [microtransit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microtransit), and [paratransit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paratransit) services. The [Julia M. Carson Transit Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_M._Carson_Transit_Center) serves as the downtown hub for 27 of its 31 [fixed routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transit_bus).[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-transport-466] In 2020, IndyGo's fleet of 212 buses provided about 4.8 million passenger trips (compared with pre-[COVID-19 pandemic](63509217.md) ridership of 9.2 million in 2019).[^cite_note-477] The [Central Indiana Regional Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Indiana_Regional_Transportation_Authority) is a quasi-governmental agency that organizes regional [car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carpool) and [vanpools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanpools) and operates three public [shuttle buses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shuttle_bus) connecting Indianapolis to employment centers in suburban [Plainfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plainfield,_Indiana) and [Whitestown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitestown,_Indiana).[^cite_note-478]

[Indianapolis International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianapolis_International_Airport)'s Colonel H.
